<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For a projective ℚ-factorial dlt pair with effective rational boundary over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, we prove that the adjoint has positive Iitaka dimension whenever a nonzero effective Cartier multiple is supported on the coefficient-one boundary and restricts to a semiample line bundle on its whole reduced support. This gives log abundance after nonvanishing in dimension at most four over ℂ.

- **claim-supported-boundary-lifting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bl-thm-supported)): Let $(V,C)$ be a normal projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt pair over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, with $C$ an effective rational boundary, and put $A=K_V+C$. Suppose that $$0\ne G\geq0,\qquad G\sim qA,\qquad \operatorname{Supp}G\subseteq\operatorname{Supp}\lfloor C\rfloor,$$ where $G$ is Cartier and $q>0$ is a sufficiently divisible integer. If $\mathcal O_V(G)\|_{G_{\mathrm{red}}}$ is semiample, then $\kappa(V,A)>0$.
  - Explanation: Generation on the whole reduced support of a nonzero effective adjoint multiple forces positive ambient Iitaka dimension. The supported representative must satisfy the stated geometric and linear-equivalence conditions.
- **claim-abundance-after-nonvanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-abundance-after-nonvanishing)): Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a projective log canonical pair of dimension at most four over $\mathbb C$, with effective rational boundary. If $D=K_X+\Delta$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and nef and $\kappa(X,D)\geq0$, then $D$ is semiample. If $\kappa(X,D)=0$, then $D\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$.
  - Explanation: A nef rational log canonical adjoint in the stated dimension range becomes semiample once nonvanishing is known. Vanishing Iitaka dimension strengthens the conclusion to rational linear triviality.
- **claim-conditional-log-abundance** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-bl-thm-conditional)): Assume that every smooth connected projective complex variety $W$ with pseudo-effective $K_W$, in every dimension, has a nonzero section of $mK_W$ for some $m>0$. Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a normal projective log canonical pair over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, with $\Delta$ an effective rational boundary and $D=K_X+\Delta$ a nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor. Then $D$ is semiample.
  - Explanation: The all-dimensional smooth canonical nonvanishing premise yields semiampleness for nef rational log canonical adjoints. The premise includes pseudo-effective canonical divisors that are not nef.
- **claim-fourfold-log-abundance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-full-abundance)): Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a normal projective log canonical pair of dimension at most four over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. If $\Delta$ is an effective rational boundary and $D=K_X+\Delta$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and nef, then $D$ is semiample. If $\kappa(X,D)=0$, then $D\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$.
  - Explanation: Combining abundance after nonvanishing with the companion nonvanishing result gives full rational log abundance in the stated range over algebraically closed characteristic-zero fields.
- **claim-complex-supported-assertion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bl-prop-complex-supported)): Let $V$ be normal and projective over $\mathbb C$, and let $C$ be an effective rational boundary. Suppose that $K_V$ and every component of $C$ are individually $\mathbb Q$-Cartier, that $(V,C)$ is lc, and that it is klt outside $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\lfloor C\rfloor$. If a nonzero effective Cartier divisor $G\sim q(K_V+C)$ has support in $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\lfloor C\rfloor$, for a sufficiently divisible positive integer $q$, and $\mathcal O_V(G)\|_{G_{\mathrm{red}}}$ is semiample on the whole reduced scheme, then $\kappa(V,K_V+C)>0$.
  - Explanation: The complex lifting argument needs individual Cartier witnesses, log canonicity, and klt singularities away from the floor. These weaker hypotheses support the subsequent field transfer.
- **claim-reduced-crepant-cover** (construction, [source](#stmt-bl-cov-reduced-cover)): The divisor $E=(y=0)$ on $Z$ is reduced and Cartier, with ideal $I=(y)=\mathcal O_Z(-E)$. Put $H_Z=\pi^*p^*H$ and $C_{0,Z}=\pi^*p^*C_0$. With compatible canonical divisors one has $$\label{bl:cov:crepant} K_Z+E+H_Z+C_{0,Z}=\pi^*(K_P+p^*C).$$ The pair $(Z,E+H_Z+C_{0,Z})$ is lc and is klt off $E+H_Z$. The map $E\to S_P$ induces an equivariant projective morphism $g:E\to B$.
  - Explanation: The full normalized root cover has a reduced Cartier zero divisor. Its crepant boundary identity controls singularities and provides the projective boundary morphism used to detect obstructions.
- **claim-logarithmic-form-and-residue** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bl-cov-poles)): The tautological form and its residue satisfy $$\label{bl:cov:residue-form} \sigma\in H^0\bigl(Y,\omega_Y(R+D_h)\bigr),\qquad \Omega=\mathop{\mathrm{res}}_R(\sigma)\in H^0\bigl(R,\omega_R(D_h)\bigr).$$ Here $\omega_R$ is the absolute dualizing sheaf of the reduced simple-normal-crossing divisor $R$. Both forms have weight $a$ and deck character $\chi$.
  - Explanation: Log canonicity bounds the poles of the tautological form on the resolution. Residue preserves its character and frame weight, while poles over the additional boundary remain allowed.
- **claim-split-residue-injection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bl-lem-residue-injection)): Multiplication by $\Omega$ after pullback induces a split morphism $$\label{bl:cov:derived-split} \mathcal O_E\longrightarrow \mathbf R(q_R)_*\omega_R(D_h)$$ in the derived category of $\mathcal O_E$-modules. Consequently, for every $i\geq0$, it induces an injection $$\label{bl:cov:injections} R^ig_*\mathcal O_E\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow R^ih_*\omega_R(D_h),\qquad e'\longmapsto\Omega e'.$$ In particular this holds for $i=1$. The map increases pullback weight by $a$.
  - Explanation: Character projection supplies a retraction of residue insertion in the derived category. The resulting cohomology injections retain all obstruction classes and increase their frame weight.
- **claim-whole-lowest-filtered-piece** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bl-lem-lowest-piece)): With the normalization above, $$\begin{aligned} F_{<0}\mathcal M&=0,& F_0\mathcal M&=(\iota,h)_*\omega_R(D_h), \label{bl:hod:lowest-source}\\ F_{<0}\mathcal N&=0,& F_0\mathcal N&=R^1h_*\omega_R(D_h). \label{bl:hod:lowest-target} \end{aligned}$$ The equality for $F_0\mathcal N$ identifies it with a subsheaf of $\mathcal N$. It is induced by simple graph residues and the inclusion of the last term of the relative de Rham complex. The latter inclusion commutes with right differentiation in base coordinates.
  - Explanation: Simple graph residues and projective strictness identify the lowest filtered direct-image piece with the complete coherent residue cohomology sheaf. The insertion respects right differentiation in base coordinates.
- **claim-lowest-symbol-kernel-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bl-lem-symbol-vanishing)): Suppose $F_{<0}\mathcal N_0=0$. Then $$\label{bl:hod:symbol-vanishing} \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\!\left(H_0, \ker\left[F_0\mathcal N_0\otimes T_{T_0} \longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_1^F\mathcal N_0\right]\right)=0.$$ The arrow is the symbol of the right differential-operator action.
  - Explanation: Kodaira–Saito vanishing rules out a homomorphism from an ample line bundle into the lowest symbol kernel on a smooth projective base.
- **claim-positive-frame-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bl-prop-frame-positivity)): For the filtered module $\mathcal N$ of <a href="#bl:hod:modules" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:hod:modules">[bl:hod:modules]</a>, the following assertions hold.* 1. *If $v\in H^0(B,F_0\mathcal N\otimes T_B)$ is homogeneous of weight $m>0$ and has zero symbol in $\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_1^F\mathcal N$, then its image in $F_0\mathcal N\otimes p_B^*T_T$ is zero.* 2. *If $s\in H^0(B,F_0\mathcal N)$ is homogeneous of weight $m$, then its actual right differential-operator action satisfies $$\label{bl:frm:euler-action} s\varepsilon=-ms.$$ The same assertion is valid on invariant bundle charts.
  - Explanation: Positive weight eliminates the horizontal projection of a tensor with zero symbol. The actual Euler action then determines the vertical contribution, rather than merely its symbol.
- **claim-filtered-transverse-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bl-frm-comparison)): For every $j$ there are natural equivariant isomorphisms $$\label{bl:frm:filtered-comparison} F_j\mathcal N' \simeq \eta^*F_j\mathcal N\otimes\omega_{B'/B}.$$ Under these isomorphisms, symbol action transforms by the tangent map $d\eta:T_{B'}\to\eta^*T_B$.
  - Explanation: A transverse finite flat power cover preserves the filtration with the relative canonical twist appropriate to right differential modules. Symbols transform through the tangent map.
- **claim-all-finite-order-lifting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bl-prop-lifting)): For every $k\geq1$, the truncation map $$\label{bl:lift:successive} g_*(\mathcal O_Z/I^{k+1})\longrightarrow g_*(\mathcal O_Z/I^k)$$ is surjective as a map of additive sheaves on $B$. More generally, for all integers $j<d$, the maps $$g_*(I^j/I^{d+1})\longrightarrow g_*(I^j/I^d), \qquad g_*(I^j/I^d)\longrightarrow g_*(I^j/I^{j+1})$$ are surjective. Each of these surjections is also surjective on sections over every affine open subset of $B$.
  - Explanation: The obstruction derivation vanishes through residue insertion, horizontal symbol vanishing, and the actual Euler action. This gives successive and leading-layer surjections, including signed ideal powers and affine sections.
- **claim-descended-layer-section-growth** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bl-lem-descent-growth)): For $j\in\mathbb Z$, define the divisorial sheaf and its layer by $$J_j^V=\mathcal O_V\bigl(-\lceil jG/r\rceil\bigr), \qquad \mathcal B_j=J_j^V/J_{j+1}^V, \qquad Q_j=f_*\mathcal B_j.$$ The sheaves $\mathcal B_j$ are coherent on $S$, and for every $d>j$ the map $$\label{bl:lift:descent-surjection} f_*(J_j^V/J_d^V)\longrightarrow Q_j$$ is surjective as a map of additive sheaves on $T$. Here the left side is pushed along $\|f\|$ on its topological support $\|S\|$. Furthermore, $$h^0\bigl(V,\mathcal O_V(NG)/\mathcal O_V\bigr)\longrightarrow\infty \qquad\text{as }N\longrightarrow\infty.$$
  - Explanation: Finite-cover invariants and the frame degree-zero part descend the lifted layers to divisorial quotients. Periodic positive twists give unbounded sections while higher cohomology remains bounded, including when the boundary image is a point.
- **claim-minimal-model-section-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bl-lem-abundance-model-comparison)): Let $(W,\Delta_W)$ be a projective lc rational pair and let $(V,\Delta_V)$ be a $\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt log minimal model. Use the log birational model convention in which divisors extracted on $V$ have coefficient one. On a common resolution $\alpha:U\to W$, $\beta:U\to V$, with compatible canonical divisors, $$\alpha^*(K_W+\Delta_W) =\beta^*(K_V+\Delta_V)+E, \qquad E\geq0,\quad E\text{ is }\beta\text{-exceptional}. \label{bl:eq:abundance-model-comparison}$$ Consequently the adjoints have the same section spaces in sufficiently divisible degrees, and hence the same Kodaira dimension.
  - Explanation: The adjoint comparison introduces an effective divisor exceptional over the minimal model. This divisor creates no extra sections, so divisible section spaces and Kodaira dimension agree.
- **claim-whole-floor-semiampleness** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-bl-lem-abundance-floor)): Assume abundance for projective lc rational pairs of dimension less than $d$. If $(V,C)$ is a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt pair of dimension $d$ and $A=K_V+C$ is nef, then the actual restricted rational line bundle $A\|_P$, where $P=\lfloor C\rfloor$, is semiample. Its restriction to any reduced union of components of $P$ is also semiample.
  - Explanation: Lower-dimensional abundance and conductor gluing generate a multiple of the actual adjoint line bundle on the whole reduced floor. Generation also persists on a selected reduced union of floor components.
- **claim-abundance-induction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-bl-prop-abundance-induction)): Fix $d\geq1$ and assume the following two statements over $\mathbb C$:* 1. *every projective lc rational pair of dimension at most $d$ whose adjoint has an effective $\mathbb Q$-linearly equivalent divisor has an ordinary log minimal model;* 2. *every nef projective lc rational adjoint in dimension less than $d$ is semiample.* *Then, for a projective lc rational pair $(X,B_X)$ of dimension $d$, $$D=K_X+B_X\text{ nef},\qquad \kappa(X,D)\geq0 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad D\text{ semiample}.$$ If $\kappa(X,D)=0$, then $D\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$.
  - Explanation: Effective ordinary minimal models and full lower-dimensional abundance imply abundance after nonvanishing in the current dimension. The proof separates the zero-Iitaka case, potentially non-nef fiber adjoints, and abundance on log canonical centers.
- **claim-specified-data-field-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bl-lem-field-transfer)): Let $\mathbf k$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. A finite collection of projective varieties over $\mathbf k$, rational divisors, specified Cartier multiples, morphisms, line bundles, sections, and identities between these data can be defined over a countable algebraically closed subfield $\mathbf k_0\subset\mathbf k$. The field $\mathbf k_0$ admits an embedding into $\mathbb C$.* *For the data considered here, one can include a log resolution and its discrepancy identities in this descent. Log canonicity, and klt singularities outside a specified reduced boundary, then hold after extension from $\mathbf k_0$ to either $\mathbf k$ or $\mathbb C$. For a rational Cartier divisor on a projective variety over $\mathbf k_0$, nefness, Kodaira dimension, and generation of any fixed Cartier multiple are unchanged under algebraically closed field extension.
  - Explanation: Finite presentation descends the specified geometric and divisor data to a countable algebraically closed field admitting a complex embedding. Discrepancy identities, nefness, section spaces, and generation of the same fixed Cartier multiple transfer under extension.
- **claim-abundance-after-nonvanishing-fields** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-abundance-after-nonvanishing-fields)): Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a projective lc rational pair of dimension at most four over an algebraically closed field $\mathbf k$ of characteristic zero. Suppose that $D=K_X+\Delta$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and nef and $\kappa(X,D)\geq0$. Then $D$ is semiample, and if $\kappa(X,D)=0$, then $D\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$.
  - Explanation: The complex abundance conclusion transfers to the original algebraically closed characteristic-zero field through generation of a fixed multiple. In the zero-Iitaka case its generating section trivializes that multiple.
- **claim-curve-base-numerical-dimension-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nu2-thm-alternative)): Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective complex fourfold. If its canonical divisor $K_X$ is nef and $\kappa(X,K_X)=0$, then $\nu(K_X)\ne2$.
  - Explanation: A separate conormal–period argument excludes numerical dimension two for the actual nef canonical divisor of a smooth complex fourfold with zero Kodaira dimension. It does not require an Euler-characteristic hypothesis.
- **claim-curve-hodge-supported-trace-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nu2-lem-hodge-test)): Let $p:Y\to T$ be projective, where $T$ is a smooth complex algebraic curve and $Y$ is a reduced, pure three-dimensional, Cohen–Macaulay Du Bois variety. Components of $Y$ may map to points. On an open $T^\circ$ with flat Du Bois surface fibers, coherent base change and topological local systems, put $\mathbb H=R^1p_*\mathbb Q$. Then $$\label{nu2:eq:hodge} R^1p_*\omega_Y=\omega_T\otimes F^0\mathcal H^\vee, \qquad F^1\mathcal H^\vee=0 \quad\text{on }T^\circ,$$ where $\mathcal H=\mathbb H\otimes\mathcal O$. For a flat local section $\alpha$ of $\mathbb H\otimes\mathbb C$, let $\alpha_{\mathcal O}$ be its image in $R^1p_*\mathcal O_Y$. The functional corresponding to $s$ is $\alpha\mapsto\langle s/dt,\alpha_{\mathcal O}\rangle$ under relative Serre duality. The variation is graded-polarizable and admissible, with quasi-unipotent monodromy. A section $s$ extending over a missing parameter obeys $$\label{nu2:eq:one-pole} \langle s/dt,\alpha_{\mathcal O}\rangle =O\bigl(\|t\|^{-1}(1+\|\log\|t\|\|)^N\bigr)$$ in flat frames on bounded-argument sectors. Moreover, a germ of $R^1p_*\omega_Y$ supported at a point is zero if its absolute trace annihilates the image of $H_c^2(Y_U,\mathbb C)\to H_c^2(Y_U,\mathcal O_Y)$ on one sufficiently small disk $U$. Neither assertion at the missing point requires a flat map or a reduced Du Bois central fiber.
  - Explanation: The good-locus dualizing direct image is identified with a Hodge bundle and extending sections satisfy a controlled pole estimate. A separate absolute trace test detects point-supported germs even when the central fiber lacks the good-locus properties.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bl-prop-frame-positivity",
        "exact_quote": "For the filtered module $\\mathcal N$ of <a href=\"#bl:hod:modules\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"bl:hod:modules\">[bl:hod:modules]</a>, the following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *If $v\\in H^0(B,F_0\\mathcal N\\otimes T_B)$ is homogeneous of weight $m>0$ and has zero symbol in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}_1^F\\mathcal N$, then its image in $F_0\\mathcal N\\otimes p_B^*T_T$ is zero.*\n\n\n2.  *If $s\\in H^0(B,F_0\\mathcal N)$ is homogeneous of weight $m$, then its actual right differential-operator action satisfies $$\\label{bl:frm:euler-action}\n     s\\varepsilon=-ms.$$ The same assertion is valid on invariant bundle charts.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.7"
      },

More generally, for all integers $j<d$, the maps $$g_*(I^j/I^{d+1})\\longrightarrow g_*(I^j/I^d),\n \\qquad\n g_*(I^j/I^d)\\longrightarrow g_*(I^j/I^{j+1})$$ are surjective.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bl-lem-abundance-model-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(W,\\Delta_W)$ be a projective lc rational pair and let $(V,\\Delta_V)$ be a $\\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt log minimal model. Use the log birational model convention in which divisors extracted on $V$ have coefficient one. On a common resolution $\\alpha:U\\to W$, $\\beta:U\\to V$, with compatible canonical divisors, $$\\alpha^*(K_W+\\Delta_W)\n   =\\beta^*(K_V+\\Delta_V)+E,\n \\qquad E\\geq0,\\quad E\\text{ is }\\beta\\text{-exceptional}.\n \\label{bl:eq:abundance-model-comparison}$$ Consequently the adjoints have the same section spaces in sufficiently divisible degrees, and hence the same Kodaira dimension.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.7"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bl-lem-abundance-model-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(W,\\Delta_W)$ be a projective lc rational pair and let $(V,\\Delta_V)$ be a $\\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt log minimal model. Use the log birational model convention in which divisors extracted on $V$ have coefficient one.           "source_id": "PM-034.7"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $(W,\\Delta_W)$ be a projective lc rational pair and let $(V,\\Delta_V)$ be a $\\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt log minimal model.         "anchor": "stmt-bl-prop-abundance-induction",
        "exact_quote": "Then, for a projective lc rational pair $(X,B_X)$ of dimension $d$, $$D=K_X+B_X\\text{ nef},\\qquad \\kappa(X,D)\\geq0\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad D\\text{ semiample}.$$ If $\\kappa(X,D)=0$, then $D\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.7"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bl-prop-abundance-induction",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $d\\geq1$ and assume the following two statements over $\\mathbb C$:*\n\n1.  *every projective lc rational pair of dimension at most $d$ whose adjoint has an effective $\\mathbb Q$-linearly equivalent divisor has an ordinary log minimal model;*\n\n2.  *every nef projective lc rational adjoint in dimension less than $d$ is semiample.*\n\n*Then, for a projective lc rational pair $(X,B_X)$ of dimension $d$, $$D=K_X+B_X\\text{ nef},\\qquad \\kappa(X,D)\\geq0\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad D\\text{ semiample}.$$ If $\\kappa(X,D)=0$, then $D\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.7"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $d\\geq1$ and assume the following two statements over $\\mathbb C$:*\n\n1.  *every projective lc rational pair of dimension at most $d$ whose adjoint has an effective $\\mathbb Q$-linearly equivalent divisor has an ordinary log minimal model;*\n\n2.  *every nef projective lc rational adjoint in dimension less than $d$ is semiample.*\n\n*Then, for a projective lc rational pair $(X,B_X)$ of dimension $d$, $$D=K_X+B_X\\text{ nef},\\qquad \\kappa(X,D)\\geq0\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad D\\text{ semiample}.$$ If $\\kappa(X,D)=0$, then $D\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$.",

A finite collection of projective varieties over $\\mathbf k$, rational divisors, specified Cartier multiples, morphisms, line bundles, sections, and identities between these data can be defined over a countable algebraically closed subfield $\\mathbf k_0\\subset\\mathbf k$. The field $\\mathbf k_0$ admits an embedding into $\\mathbb C$.*\n\n*For the data considered here, one can include a log resolution and its discrepancy identities in this descent. Log canonicity, and klt singularities outside a specified reduced boundary, then hold after extension from $\\mathbf k_0$ to either $\\mathbf k$ or $\\mathbb C$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Effective rational boundary** ([source](#pm-h-7)): All boundaries are effective with rational coefficients in $[0,1]$. An adjoint is an actual $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor, and $\sim_{\mathbb Q}$ denotes rational linear equivalence. A rational Cartier divisor is semiample if a positive Cartier multiple is generated by global sections; its Iitaka dimension is the dimension of the images of all sufficiently divisible nonempty complete systems, with value $-\infty$ if no such system exists. For a nef divisor $D$ on a projective $d$-fold, $$\nu(D)=\max\{j:D^jH^{d-j}>0\}$$ for an ample divisor $H$; these intersections may be computed on a smooth resolution. We use log discrepancies, so coefficient-one divisors have log discrepancy zero. Standard conventions for lc, klt, dlt and dlt adjunction follow [[Kollar2013](#ref-Kollar2013)].
- **Adjoint and rational linear equivalence** ([source](#pm-h-7)): An adjoint is an actual $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor, and $\sim_{\mathbb Q}$ denotes rational linear equivalence.
- **Semiampleness and Iitaka dimension** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A rational Cartier divisor is semiample if a positive Cartier multiple is generated by global sections; its Iitaka dimension is the dimension of the images of all sufficiently divisible nonempty complete systems, with value $-\infty$ if no such system exists.
- **Numerical dimension of a nef divisor** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For a nef divisor $D$ on a projective $d$-fold, $$\nu(D)=\max\{j:D^jH^{d-j}>0\}$$ for an ample divisor $H$; these intersections may be computed on a smooth resolution.
- **Pullback weight** ([source](#pm-h-9)): All weights below are pullback weights: a tensor $u$ has weight $m$ if $\lambda^*u=\lambda^m u$.
- **Filtered boundary-graph modules** ([source](#bl:hod:modules)): Define $$\label{bl:hod:modules} \mathcal M=\mathcal H^{b+1}_{[Q]} \bigl(\omega_{Y\times B}(*D_h)\bigr), \qquad \mathcal N=\mathcal H^1\operatorname{pr}_{B,+}\mathcal M.$$
- **Euler generator** ([source](#bl:frm:euler)): Let $\varepsilon$ be the infinitesimal generator on $B$. In a bundle chart with fiber coordinate $\beta$, $$\label{bl:frm:euler} \varepsilon=r\beta\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta}.$$
- **Descended divisorial sheaves and layers** ([source](#stmt-bl-lem-descent-growth)): For $j\in\mathbb Z$, define the divisorial sheaf and its layer by $$J_j^V=\mathcal O_V\bigl(-\lceil jG/r\rceil\bigr), \qquad \mathcal B_j=J_j^V/J_{j+1}^V, \qquad Q_j=f_*\mathcal B_j.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-obstruction-positive-weight | m | = | $a+k$ | The root indices are fixed while the infinitesimal order varies. |
| q-euler-right-action | s\varepsilon | = | $-ms$ | Homogeneous sections on the frame bundle or invariant bundle charts. |
| q-double-error-order | 2k | ≥ | $k+1$ | Positive infinitesimal orders in the lifting induction. |
| q-fixed-higher-cohomology-bound | h^i(T,\mathcal A_N) | ≤ | $C_i$ | The constants are independent of the number of accumulated positive layers. |
| q-curve-layer-euler-polynomial | \chi(Q(b)) | = | $\chi(Q)+b\operatorname{rk}Q$ | All integral twists of a fixed coherent sheaf. |
| q-quadratic-growth-coefficient | coefficient of $n^2$ in the section lower bound | = | $\tfrac12\sum_{\ell=0}^{r-1}\operatorname{rk}F_\ell$ | The cover index and coherent layer ranks are fixed while the Cartier multiple increases. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-reduced-crepant-cover** [claim](#stmt-bl-cov-reduced-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bl-cov-reduced-cover): Normalize the full Kummer algebra, calculate divisorial orders, and transfer discrepancies through finite maps. -> Reduced Cartier cover boundary with a projective map to the frame target.
- **step-tautological-poles** [claim](#stmt-bl-cov-poles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bl-cov-poles): Take the full adjoint root cover and an equivariant resolution, then bound pole orders by log discrepancies and apply residue adjunction. -> Logarithmic top form and residue with the required positive weight and deck character.
- **step-residue-splitting** [claim](#stmt-bl-lem-residue-injection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bl-lem-residue-injection): Identify the character eigensheaf, apply relative vanishing, compare thickened and reduced residue quotients, and project to the character summand. -> Split residue insertion and injections on all higher direct images.
- **step-lowest-filtered-piece** [claim](#stmt-bl-lem-lowest-piece) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bl-lem-lowest-piece): Compute simple normal crossing graph residues and use projective-support filtered strictness on the relative de Rham complex. -> The whole coherent residue cohomology sheaf as the lowest filtered direct-image piece.
- **step-symbol-kernel-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-bl-lem-symbol-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bl-lem-symbol-vanishing): Apply Kodaira–Saito vanishing to the graded de Rham complex whose surviving negative-degree term computes the twisted symbol kernel. -> No ample line maps into the lowest symbol kernel.
- **step-transverse-filtered-comparison** [claim](#stmt-bl-frm-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bl-frm-comparison): Choose a transverse finite flat power cover and compare graph fractions, relative canonical factors, and filtered direct images. -> Equivariant filtered comparison and transformation rule for symbols.
- **step-frame-positivity** [claim](#stmt-bl-prop-frame-positivity) / [proof](#pm-h-18): Transport the horizontal symbol by the determinant-twisted adjugate and descend to the projective quotient; calculate the actual Euler action separately on an étale fiber cover. -> Horizontal projection vanishes and actual vertical action is the Euler eigenvalue.
- **step-finite-order-lifting** [claim](#stmt-bl-prop-lifting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bl-prop-lifting): Factor connecting maps through derivations, establish the graph residue identity for arbitrary boundary functions, and annihilate horizontal and vertical obstruction parts. -> All successive and leading-layer truncation maps are surjective, including affine sections and signed powers.
- **step-descent-growth** [claim](#stmt-bl-lem-descent-growth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bl-lem-descent-growth): Take finite-cover invariants and frame degree zero, identify ceiling layers, and count their periodic positive twists using finite filtrations and Serre vanishing. -> Unbounded sections of the quotient by the structure sheaf with uniformly bounded higher cohomology.
- **step-complex-supported-positivity** [claim](#stmt-bl-prop-complex-supported) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bl-lem-descent-growth-2): Pass from quotient sections to ambient sections with a fixed cohomological loss, then use a nonconstant ratio of sections. -> Positive Iitaka dimension in the weaker complex Cartier-witness formulation.
- **step-model-section-comparison** [claim](#stmt-bl-lem-abundance-model-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bl-lem-abundance-model-comparison): Use the negativity lemma and the extracted-boundary convention to show the adjoint difference is effective and exceptional over the minimal model. -> Equality of divisible section spaces and Kodaira dimension.
- **step-whole-floor-generation** [claim](#stmt-bl-lem-abundance-floor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bl-lem-abundance-floor): Apply whole-floor adjunction, lower-dimensional abundance on normalization pairs, and semi-log-canonical conductor gluing. -> Semiampleness on the whole reduced floor and on any selected reduced union.
- **step-abundance-induction** [claim](#stmt-bl-prop-abundance-induction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bl-prop-abundance-induction): Contradict a nonzero effective representative in the zero-Iitaka case; compare non-nef Iitaka-fiber adjoints with effective minimal models; prove numerical triviality on fibers and log abundance on centers. -> Semiampleness after nonvanishing and rational linear triviality in the zero-Iitaka case.
- **step-complex-abundance-application** [claim](#stmt-thm-abundance-after-nonvanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bl-prop-abundance-induction-2): Supply classical lower-dimensional abundance and effective lc minimal-model existence in the stated range to the induction proposition. -> Complex abundance after nonvanishing in dimension at most four.
- **step-specified-field-transfer** [claim](#stmt-bl-lem-field-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bl-lem-field-transfer): Descend finite presentation and discrepancy identities to a countable algebraically closed field, compare sections by flat base change, and detect generation through the evaluation cokernel. -> Transfer of singularity conditions, nefness, Kodaira dimension, and fixed-degree generation.
- **step-supported-theorem-fields** [claim](#stmt-bl-thm-supported) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bl-lem-field-transfer-2): Descend the supported pair with its individual Cartier witnesses and generated boundary multiple, apply the weaker complex assertion, and transfer section spaces back. -> Supported boundary lifting over any algebraically closed characteristic-zero field.
- **step-nonvanishing-abundance-fields** [claim](#stmt-cor-abundance-after-nonvanishing-fields) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-abundance-after-nonvanishing-fields): Apply complex abundance to the descended original pair and transfer the precise generated Cartier multiple; trivialize it when its section space has the stated dimension. -> Abundance after nonvanishing over algebraically closed characteristic-zero fields.
- **step-conditional-all-dimensional-abundance** [claim](#stmt-bl-thm-conditional) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bl-prop-abundance-induction-3): Use smooth canonical nonvanishing to obtain lc nonvanishing and ordinary minimal models; pass from real to rational linear effectivity, induct on dimension, and transfer fixed-degree generation. -> Conditional semiampleness of all nef rational lc adjoints in characteristic zero.
- **step-full-fourfold-abundance** [claim](#stmt-thm-full-abundance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-abundance-after-nonvanishing-fields-2): Use the companion original-variety nonvanishing result to supply a first section, apply abundance after nonvanishing, and transfer generation back to the original field. -> Full rational log abundance in the stated dimension range.
- **step-curve-hodge-trace-test** [claim](#stmt-nu2-lem-hodge-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nu2-lem-hodge-test): Combine the Du Bois comparison, filtered proper duality, strict specializability, and the perverse-stalk spectral sequence with compatibility of coherent and topological trace. -> Hodge identification, pole estimate, and detection of point-supported germs.
- **step-curve-base-alternative** [claim](#stmt-nu2-thm-alternative) / [proof](#nu2:subsec:count): Construct an effective dlt boundary pencil, form compatible root covers, eliminate period and supported obstructions, and count finite invariant layers to contradict zero Kodaira dimension. -> Exclusion of numerical dimension two by positive quadratic section growth.

<!-- PM:END pm.roadmap.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-supported-ambient-positivity**: A nonzero effective Cartier multiple of the adjoint supported on the coefficient-one boundary forces positive ambient Iitaka dimension when its restriction is semiample on the whole reduced support.
- **invariant-abundance-after-nonvanishing**: Within the stated dimension range, a nef rational Cartier log canonical adjoint with nonnegative Iitaka dimension is semiample; zero Iitaka dimension further implies rational linear triviality.
- **invariant-conditional-global-premise**: All-dimensional abundance is conditional on smooth canonical nonvanishing for every pseudo-effective canonical divisor in the stated complex projective class, including canonical divisors that are not nef.
- **invariant-complete-obstruction-sheaf**: The lowest filtered piece retains the entire coherent residue cohomology sheaf, including sections supported on smaller strata; neither local freeness nor restriction to a variation locus is required.
- **invariant-horizontal-symbol-and-vertical-action**: Positive homogeneous weight and a vanishing first symbol eliminate the horizontal projection. The remaining vertical obstruction must be tested using the actual right Euler action, keeping coefficients before derivatives.
- **invariant-finite-order-lifting**: Successive truncations and signed-power leading-layer maps are surjective as additive sheaves, with surjectivity on sections over affine opens. This does not require an ambient morphism from a thickening to the base.
- **invariant-specified-generation-transfer**: Generation of a fixed Cartier multiple transfers through algebraically closed field extensions using base change of the original line bundle and its evaluation map. Auxiliary complex minimal models need not descend.
- **invariant-curve-base-canonical-scope**: The curve-base alternative excludes numerical dimension two for a smooth connected projective complex fourfold with nef canonical divisor and zero Kodaira dimension.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-componentwise-generation**: Replace semiampleness on the whole reduced support by separate generation on its components. -> Separately chosen sections may fail to agree along intersections, so they do not establish generation of the restricted line bundle on the reduced scheme.
- **anti-use-numerical-representative**: Use a merely numerically equivalent effective divisor as the supported adjoint representative. -> The root of the adjoint tensor requires the given rational linear equivalence; numerical equivalence does not supply that identification.
- **anti-use-unconditional-all-dimensional-abundance**: Treat the conditional abundance theorem as an unconditional conclusion in every dimension. -> The reduction assumes smooth canonical nonvanishing in every dimension, including the pseudo-effective non-nef case.
- **anti-use-nonvanishing-from-after-nonvanishing**: Use abundance after nonvanishing to obtain the first adjoint section. -> The theorem assumes nonnegative Iitaka dimension; its proof does not supply the independent first section needed for the full abundance corollary.
- **anti-use-discard-small-supports**: Check the residue obstruction only on the smooth variation locus or assume the relevant coherent sheaf is locally free. -> Obstructions on smaller supports can disappear from that restricted test while remaining nonzero in the complete coherent sheaf.
- **anti-use-symbol-as-euler-operator**: Infer the actual Euler eigenvalue solely from a vanishing vertical symbol, or commute coefficients through right derivatives. -> The symbol does not determine the actual operator action, and commuting coefficients introduces a divergence correction.
- **anti-use-formal-convergence**: Interpret all-order finite lifting as a convergent formal splitting or as an ambient extension of the boundary morphism. -> The construction pushes additive sheaves along the boundary's underlying map and counts finite filtrations; it does not establish either asserted extension.
- **anti-use-effective-index-or-runtime**: Read qualitative semiampleness as a computable uniform generated multiple or an algorithm with a certified running time. -> The abundance conclusion gives existence of a generated Cartier multiple without a uniform index or a finite computational procedure.
- **anti-use-generalised-canonical-alternative**: Apply the curve-base alternative to arbitrary singular pairs or to a canonical divisor with no nonzero pluricanonical section. -> The alternative assumes a smooth complex fourfold and zero Kodaira dimension; its effective divisor comes from the section already supplied by that hypothesis.
- **anti-use-factoriality-descent**: Assume that global rational factoriality survives the descent and complex extension used in the proof. -> The transfer argument moves individual Cartier witnesses and discrepancy identities and expressly avoids a general factoriality-preservation assertion.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The paper proves qualitative existence and geometric lifting for arbitrary projective pairs, using coherent cohomology, mixed Hodge modules, minimal models and induction through arbitrary finite orders. These are not finite constructive computational contracts. The abundance discussion explicitly states: “It applies to arbitrary rational boundary coefficients and supplies a qualitative generated Cartier multiple, with no uniform index.” The infinitesimal lifting proposition quantifies over every finite order, while the section-growth argument chooses its generation threshold by Serre vanishing. A finite arithmetic check of layer identities would not decide the geometric hypotheses or certify the claimed abundance conclusions.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Supported lifting proves existence and growth of geometric sections. It supplies no executable procedure for extending software objects or resolving application errors. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The curve-base alternative concerns canonical divisors on smooth projective complex fourfolds. Its geometric obstruction argument does not construct a simulation model or an update rule for game state. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Semiampleness guarantees that some positive Cartier multiple is generated by global sections. The paper gives no uniform index or procedure for finding that multiple, so it does not define a terminating generator tool. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The supported theorem establishes positive Iitaka dimension under geometric hypotheses. It does not specify finite input encodings, a section-search algorithm or a computational complexity bound. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The descended layers are coherent sheaves, while their filtered additive pushforwards need not be coherent modules. These mathematical objects do not supply a storage representation or supported data-structure operations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The transverse comparison identifies filtered geometric direct images after a finite cover. It contains no computational kernel, arithmetic workload or hardware execution model that could support accelerator design. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The cyclic covers and deck characters support a split residue insertion in a derived category. They provide no cryptographic construction, adversarial model or security guarantee. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Positive frame weight controls a differential symbol and an Euler action on a filtered geometric module. It is unrelated to learned parameters and supplies no training objective, inference procedure or generalisation bound. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The abundance induction assumes ordinary log minimal models and lower-dimensional semiampleness. Minimality here is birational geometry; the proposition supplies neither an optimisation objective nor a finite feasible-set solver. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Whole-floor semiampleness uses adjunction and gluing on a reduced boundary scheme. Compatibility along its double locus does not define a communication protocol, replica agreement rule or fault-tolerance contract. |
| other (Algebraic geometry and mathematical verification) | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The results directly support theoretical reasoning about abundance and field transfer. Their existence proofs can guide a mathematical verification specification, but the supplied corpus does not give a finite executable construction that certifies the full geometric hypotheses. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-supported-geometric-regime** (hypothesis): Supported positivity requires the stated normal projective factorial dlt pair over an algebraically closed characteristic-zero field, with effective rational boundary. Blocked use: ['Apply supported boundary lifting to arbitrary nonprojective varieties, positive-characteristic pairs, or pairs without the required singularity and Cartier hypotheses.']
- **limit-nonzero-supported-linear-representative** (hypothesis): The effective Cartier representative must be nonzero, supported on the coefficient-one boundary, and related to the adjoint by the specified linear equivalence. Blocked use: ['Use a zero representative to conclude positive Iitaka dimension.', 'Replace the required linear equivalence with numerical equivalence.', 'Allow support outside the coefficient-one boundary without an independent extension theorem.']
- **limit-whole-reduced-support-generation** (hypothesis): Semiampleness is required on the whole reduced support with its actual line-bundle gluing. Separate componentwise generation does not establish this premise. Blocked use: ['Apply the supported theorem using incompatible generating sections on separate components.', 'Replace conductor-compatible whole-floor generation with unrelated componentwise equivalences.']
- **limit-complete-normalized-covers** (formal_scope): The normalized cyclic constructions retain every component, including when their generic algebras split. Character descent relies on the full invariant algebra. Blocked use: ['Discard a generic component and reuse the asserted invariant algebra or residue splitting without proving them for the altered cover.']
- **limit-extra-boundary-poles** (edge_case): The resolved residue construction must retain possible poles over the additional coefficient-one boundary, including intersections with the selected support. Blocked use: ['Delete the extra boundary from the pole allowance.', 'Assume that the additional coefficient-one components must be disjoint from the selected support or its strata.']
- **limit-whole-coherent-obstruction-sheaves** (formal_scope): The lowest-piece and symbol-kernel arguments concern complete coherent sheaves, including sections supported on smaller strata. Testing only the variation locus can miss an obstruction. Blocked use: ['Infer vanishing of the entire obstruction from vanishing on the smooth variation locus.', 'Assume local freeness of the residue direct image.']
- **limit-transverse-filtered-comparison** (hypothesis): The filtered comparison belongs to the specified transverse finite flat cover construction and includes the relative canonical factor for right modules. Blocked use: ['Apply the comparison to an arbitrary ramified base change.', 'Omit the relative canonical factor.', 'Replace the abstract relative canonical factor with multiplication by a Jacobian that vanishes at ramification.']
- **limit-positive-weight-actual-euler-action** (hypothesis): Horizontal symbol vanishing uses positive homogeneous weight. Eliminating the vertical obstruction additionally requires the actual right Euler action and the stated ordering of coefficients. Blocked use: ['Apply the horizontal positivity assertion to a weight outside its stated positive regime.', 'Infer the Euler eigenvalue solely from a vanishing vertical symbol.', 'Commute coefficients through derivatives without accounting for the divergence correction.']
- **limit-additive-finite-truncations** (formal_scope): The lifting assertions are surjections of additive sheaves along the underlying boundary map. They do not assume a morphism from an infinitesimal thickening to the base; section surjectivity is asserted over affine opens. Blocked use: ['Treat every thickening pushforward as a coherent module over the base.', 'Assume an ambient extension of the boundary morphism.', 'Infer section surjectivity over arbitrary nonaffine opens directly from the affine assertion.']
- **limit-finite-filtration-no-convergence** (formal_scope): The section-growth arguments use finite invariant filtrations. They do not prove convergence of a formal splitting or existence of a common neighborhood for every infinitesimal order. Blocked use: ['Upgrade finite-order lifting to a convergent analytic neighborhood construction.', 'Require or claim a single neighborhood valid at all orders.']
- **limit-qualitative-generation-index** (nonconstructive): Semiampleness supplies a generated Cartier multiple qualitatively. The abundance conclusion has no uniform index, and the layer count chooses its generation threshold through Serre vanishing. Blocked use: ['Advertise a uniform computable semiampleness index.', 'Assign a certified runtime or finite stopping bound to a section-search procedure from these existence statements alone.']
- **limit-after-nonvanishing-premise-and-range** (hypothesis): Abundance after nonvanishing requires a nef rational Cartier log canonical adjoint with nonnegative Iitaka dimension in dimension at most four. Its proof does not supply the first section in dimension four. Blocked use: ['Use abundance after nonvanishing itself to obtain the initial adjoint section.', 'Apply its dimension-bounded conclusion in arbitrary dimension without the additional induction premises.', 'Drop nefness or rational Cartier conditions.']
- **limit-effective-model-induction-premises** (hypothesis): The induction proposition assumes ordinary minimal models for effective adjoints and full lower-dimensional abundance. Fiber adjoints can be non-nef before passage to their minimal models. Blocked use: ['Replace ordinary effective minimal-model existence with an unproved good-model assumption.', 'Apply lower-dimensional nef abundance directly to a non-nef original fiber adjoint.', 'Replace full lower-dimensional abundance with abundance only after nonvanishing when generating the whole floor.']
- **limit-minimal-model-section-comparison** (hypothesis): The section comparison uses the ordinary log minimal-model convention with coefficient-one extracted divisors and an effective difference exceptional over that model. Blocked use: ['Assume section spaces are preserved under arbitrary birational boundary changes.', 'Allow a nonexceptional effective difference and still assert that it creates no additional sections.']
- **limit-universal-smooth-nonvanishing-assumption** (hypothesis): The all-dimensional abundance theorem is conditional on smooth canonical nonvanishing in every dimension for pseudo-effective canonical divisors, including those that are not nef. Blocked use: ['Present the conditional theorem as unconditional abundance in all dimensions.', 'Replace its premise with nonvanishing only for nef canonical divisors or only within a bounded dimension range.']
- **limit-companion-first-section-dependency** (formal_scope): Full fourfold log abundance combines abundance after nonvanishing with an external original-variety nonvanishing result. The supported lifting proof and abundance after nonvanishing are independent of that companion's nonvanishing theorem. Blocked use: ['Attribute unconditional nonvanishing to supported lifting alone.', 'Use the full abundance corollary as the premise that proves the companion first-section input.']
- **limit-specified-cartier-data-field-transfer** (formal_scope): Field transfer concerns specified divisor data and generation of the same fixed Cartier multiple. It does not assert general preservation of factoriality or require descent of auxiliary complex minimal models. Blocked use: ['Infer global factoriality preservation from the descended individual Cartier witnesses.', 'Transfer an unspecified generated line bundle instead of the original chosen multiple.', 'Require very general points or descent of the auxiliary minimal models over the countable field.']
- **limit-canonical-fourfold-alternative** (hypothesis): The curve-base alternative concerns the actual canonical divisor of a smooth connected projective complex fourfold, with nefness and zero Kodaira dimension. Blocked use: ['Apply the alternative to arbitrary singular log pairs.', 'Use it to prove nonvanishing when no pluricanonical section is known.', 'Extend its conclusion to other dimensions or characteristics without a separate theorem.']
- **limit-good-locus-and-supported-trace** (hypothesis): The Hodge identification uses the stated flatness, Du Bois fiber, base-change and local-system conditions on the good locus. At exceptional parameters, point-supported germs require the separate absolute trace test; the missing-point assertions do not require a flat map or a reduced Du Bois central fiber. Blocked use: ['Apply the good-locus bundle identification across an exceptional parameter without justification.', 'Declare a point-supported germ zero merely because its periods vanish away from that point.', 'Apply the supported trace test without the specified total-space hypotheses or without testing the stated constant-sheaf images.']
- **limit-arithmetic-certificate-scope** (formal_scope): Exact checks of overlap-order inequalities, ceiling identities and finite layer sums do not certify the geometric hypotheses, coherent lifting, or abundance theorems. Blocked use: ['Promote arithmetic agreement to a certificate of the complete geometric theorem.', 'Treat a positive formal layer coefficient as proof that the required geometric filtration exists.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For a projective ℚ-factorial dlt pair with effective rational boundary over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, we prove that the adjoint has positive Iitaka dimension whenever a nonzero effective Cartier multiple is supported on the coefficient-one boundary and restricts to a semiample line bundle on its whole reduced support. This gives log abundance after nonvanishing in dimension at most four over ℂ.

<a id="bl:sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A nonzero section of an adjoint divisor gives an effective representative; abundance requires enough sections to define a morphism. Adjunction suggests looking first on the support of the zero divisor, where a multiple may already be generated. We prove that, when this support lies in the coefficient-one boundary, generation on the entire reduced support forces positive ambient Iitaka dimension.

<a id="stmt-bl-thm-supported"></a>
<div id="bl:thm:supported" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Supported boundary lifting). *Let $(V,C)$ be a normal projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt pair over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, with $C$ an effective rational boundary, and put $A=K_V+C$. Suppose that $$0\ne G\geq0,\qquad G\sim qA,\qquad
  \operatorname{Supp}G\subseteq\operatorname{Supp}\lfloor C\rfloor,$$ where $G$ is Cartier and $q>0$ is a sufficiently divisible integer. If $\mathcal O_V(G)|_{G_{\mathrm{red}}}$ is semiample, then $\kappa(V,A)>0$.*

</div>

The restriction is a line bundle on the whole reduced scheme $S=G_{\mathrm{red}}$. Sections on its separate components must agree along their intersections. The support inclusion may be strict: in $C=S+H+C_0$, the extra coefficient-one divisor $H$ may meet $S$ and any of its strata, while $C_0$ has coefficients less than one. No additional nefness hypothesis is needed. Curves in $S$ have nonnegative $G$-degree by semiampleness, and curves outside $S$ have nonnegative degree by effectivity. The rational coefficients of $C$ may be arbitrary in $[0,1]$.

The first application separates abundance from the task of obtaining a first section.

<a id="stmt-thm-abundance-after-nonvanishing"></a>
<div id="thm:abundance-after-nonvanishing" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Abundance after nonvanishing). *Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a projective log canonical pair of dimension at most four over $\mathbb C$, with effective rational boundary. If $D=K_X+\Delta$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and nef and $\kappa(X,D)\geq0$, then $D$ is semiample. If $\kappa(X,D)=0$, then $D\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$.*

</div>

The proof uses lower-dimensional abundance and ordinary minimal models for effective log canonical pairs. Theorem <a href="#thm:abundance-after-nonvanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:abundance-after-nonvanishing">2</a> is independent of the existence of a first section in dimension four. It applies to arbitrary rational boundary coefficients and supplies a qualitative generated Cartier multiple, with no uniform index. Section <a href="#bl:sec:fields" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:sec:fields">7</a> transfers the same conclusion to every algebraically closed field of characteristic zero.

The same supported argument gives a reduction in all dimensions.

<a id="stmt-bl-thm-conditional"></a>
<div id="bl:thm:conditional" class="theorem">

**Theorem 3** (Reduction to smooth canonical nonvanishing). *Assume that every smooth connected projective complex variety $W$ with pseudo-effective $K_W$, in every dimension, has a nonzero section of $mK_W$ for some $m>0$. Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a normal projective log canonical pair over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, with $\Delta$ an effective rational boundary and $D=K_X+\Delta$ a nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor. Then $D$ is semiample.*

</div>

The premise concerns pseudo-effective canonical divisors and includes those that are not nef. Hashizume’s reduction supplies nonvanishing and ordinary log minimal models under this premise; it does not supply the semiampleness proved here.

A separate companion provides the first section needed in dimension four.

<a id="stmt-thm-full-abundance"></a>
<div id="thm:full-abundance" class="corollary">

**Corollary 4** (Fourfold log abundance). *Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a normal projective log canonical pair of dimension at most four over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. If $\Delta$ is an effective rational boundary and $D=K_X+\Delta$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and nef, then $D$ is semiample. If $\kappa(X,D)=0$, then $D\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$.*

</div>

This resolves rational log abundance positively in the stated range, using Theorem <a href="#thm:abundance-after-nonvanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:abundance-after-nonvanishing">2</a> and the nonvanishing result in OpenAI, *Fourfold nonvanishing by minimal metrics and moving jets* (September 27, 2026), Corollary 1.2 . The proof of the supported theorem and of abundance after nonvanishing uses no result from that companion.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Boundary deformation and extension

The relation between boundary geometry and ambient sections already appears in dimension three. Kawamata’s alternative proof of Miyaoka’s numerical-dimension-one theorem constructs compatible infinitesimal boundary deformations using finite covers and Hodge theory, and then produces a moving family <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Kawamata1992](#ref-Kawamata1992), Section 4]. The question of how infinitesimal boundary data yield ambient positivity also motivates the lifting problem considered here.

Log abundance through dimension three rests on the surface and threefold theorems, including the corrected Keel–Matsuki–McKernan argument <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[KMM1994](#ref-KMM1994), [KMM2004](#ref-KMM2004), [Fujino2012](#ref-Fujino2012)] and semi-log-canonical gluing <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Fujino2000](#ref-Fujino2000)]. Liu–Xu proved abundance after nonvanishing through dimension five under the additional numerical-dimension bound $\nu\leq1$, together with a corresponding conditional reduction from smooth canonical nonvanishing in dimension $d-1$ and in numerical dimension one in dimension $d$ <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[LiuXu2025](#ref-LiuXu2025), Theorems 5.1 and 5.4]. Theorem <a href="#thm:abundance-after-nonvanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:abundance-after-nonvanishing">2</a> removes that numerical-dimension bound in dimension at most four; the lifting theorem itself holds in every dimension.

On the analytic side, Demailly–Hacon–Păun proved extension for plt pairs without requiring an ample boundary part and explained its connection with nonvanishing and good minimal models <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[DHP2013](#ref-DHP2013), Theorem 1.7 and Corollary 1.8]. Their nef plt corollary gives restriction surjectivity in the presence of a suitable effective supported representative. Chan–Choi subsequently removed one support-containment restriction in the log-smooth plt setting <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[ChanChoi2021](#ref-ChanChoi2021), Theorem 1.6.1]. Theorem <a href="#bl:thm:supported" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:thm:supported">1</a> starts with semiampleness on an entire reducible support and allows additional coefficient-one components to meet it. Compatibility at those intersections, and obstructions concentrated on smaller strata, are central to its proof.

The passage to abundance also needs distinct results about gluing and minimal models. Birkar’s effective log minimal model results provide the birational models used in the induction <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Birkar2010](#ref-Birkar2010), [Birkar2011](#ref-Birkar2011)]. Fujino–Gongyo show that semiampleness on the normalization of a semi-log-canonical pair, with its conductor boundary, descends to the original pair <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[FujinoGongyo2014](#ref-FujinoGongyo2014), Theorem 4.3]. Their nef log-abundance theorem requires abundance on the ambient variety and on the normalizations of all log canonical centers <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[FujinoGongyo2014](#ref-FujinoGongyo2014), Theorem 4.2]. Both requirements enter the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:abundance-after-nonvanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:abundance-after-nonvanishing">2</a>.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The obstruction on a finite neighborhood

After replacing $G$ by a multiple, its generated restriction gives $f:S\to\mathbb P^\ell$ with $\mathcal O_V(G)|_S\simeq f^*\mathcal O(1)$. We want to extend functions and sections through successive infinitesimal neighborhoods of $S$. There is an immediate difficulty: $f$ itself need not extend to any neighborhood. The proof must therefore measure the lifting obstruction using only the morphism on the reduced boundary.

Pass to the bundle of nonzero frames of $\mathcal O_V(G)$, where the pulled-back line bundle has a tautological trivialization. Scalar multiplication of a frame gives an action of $\mathbb G_m$. A function or form has weight $a$ if this action multiplies it by the character $\lambda^a$. Two cyclic-cover constructions, retaining all components of each normalized cover, produce a reduced Cartier divisor $E=(y=0)$. After an equivariant resolution there is a logarithmic top form $\sigma$ whose residue along the reduced resolved boundary has positive weight. Poles over the extra boundary $H$ remain present, including above its intersections with $S$.

Locally, the finite neighborhoods are defined by $(y^k)$. At the first stage where a lift from $y^k=0$ to $y^{k+1}=0$ is not yet known, differences of local lifts are multiples of $y^k$. Their coefficients form a cohomology class on $E$: this is the obstruction. Because products of two such errors vanish at that order, the connecting map on functions satisfies the Leibniz rule. Thus the obstruction is a derivation. A suitable character part of the residue map embeds its cohomology group into the cohomology of logarithmic residues.

The identity $\mathcal O_V(G)|_S=f^*\mathcal O(1)$ lifts $f$ to a map between the bundles of nonzero frames. On a chart trivializing $\mathcal O(1)$, the target of this lifted map has projective-base coordinates and an invertible frame scalar. Changing the base coordinates gives a horizontal direction; rescaling that scalar gives the vertical direction. The proof first eliminates the horizontal part of the obstruction, then uses the nonzero weight of the frame action to eliminate the vertical part. This distinction explains why positivity of the residue weight is useful.

To retain classes supported on singular fibers or smaller strata, the residue comparison is made in a filtered direct image of a Hodge module on the graph of the boundary morphism. Its lowest filtration term is the whole coherent residue cohomology sheaf. Its first symbol records the effect of differentiating in coordinates on the target of the lifted map. Saito’s strictness and Kodaira–Saito vanishing provide the required control of these filtered objects <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Saito1988](#ref-Saito1988), [Saito1990](#ref-Saito1990), [Popa2016](#ref-Popa2016)]; restricting to the smooth locus of a variation of Hodge structure would discard some of the obstructions we need to test.

A local calculation on the graph expresses the derivation and this first symbol in a single residue identity. The symbol has positive frame weight. If its horizontal part were nonzero, a finite cover transverse to that part, together with the adjugate of its differential, would produce a nonzero map from an ample line bundle into the kernel of the first symbol. Kodaira–Saito vanishing excludes that map. The remaining vertical term is the actual Euler derivative of frame scaling. The residue identity now says that a nonzero scalar, determined by its weight, times the vertical obstruction is zero. Hence that obstruction vanishes as well. Applying the same identity to arbitrary boundary functions removes the complete obstruction. This proves lifting at every finite order.

Taking finite-cover invariants and the degree-zero part of the frame action descends the lifted layers to divisorial sheaves on $V$. These layers recur with positive twists of $\mathcal O_{\mathbb P^\ell}(1)$. Their accumulated sections grow while higher cohomology stays bounded; the loss in passing from a finite neighborhood to ambient sections is bounded by a fixed cohomology group. Hence the section spaces of multiples of $G$ are unbounded, proving positive Iitaka dimension. If the boundary morphism has zero-dimensional image, the growing number of layers supplies this increase. The argument uses finite filtrations and does not require convergence of a formal neighborhood.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### From lifting to abundance and other boundary methods

For Theorem <a href="#thm:abundance-after-nonvanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:abundance-after-nonvanishing">2</a>, lower-dimensional abundance and conductor gluing generate the adjoint on the whole reduced floor. Theorem <a href="#bl:thm:supported" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:thm:supported">1</a> then excludes a nonzero effective representative in the case $\kappa=0$. For positive Iitaka dimension, effective minimal models handle the generally non-nef adjoints on the Iitaka fibers. Numerical triviality there gives abundance, including on log canonical centers, and the preceding semiampleness and descent results apply.

The complex lifting argument also works under a weaker formulation: the canonical and boundary divisors are individually $\mathbb Q$-Cartier, the pair is log canonical, and it is klt off the reduced floor. This formulation permits transfer to other algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero. One descends finitely many defining data to a countable algebraically closed field embeddable in $\mathbb C$ and transfers sections and generation by base change of the original line bundle and its evaluation map. No preservation of $\mathbb Q$-factoriality under that descent is needed.

A further method illuminates the boundary obstruction with different hypotheses. A complete conormal–period argument proves that a smooth projective complex fourfold with $K_X$ nef and $\kappa(K_X)=0$ cannot have $\nu(K_X)=2$, without an Euler-characteristic hypothesis. Its period equation and trace tests at exceptional parameters kill successive obstructions; a quadratic count of invariant layers then forces growth. This method is not needed for Theorem <a href="#bl:thm:supported" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:thm:supported">1</a>.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Reading the proof

Section <a href="#bl:sec:covers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:sec:covers">[bl:sec:covers]</a> constructs the two covers and a split residue insertion. Section <a href="#bl:sec:hodge" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:sec:hodge">3</a> identifies the lowest filtered piece and proves the symbol-kernel vanishing. Section <a href="#bl:sec:frames" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:sec:frames">4</a> separates horizontal symbols from the actual Euler action, and Section <a href="#bl:sec:lifting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:sec:lifting">5</a> uses these two conclusions to lift and count all finite layers. Section <a href="#bl:sec:abundance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:sec:abundance">6</a> proves the abundance implications over $\mathbb C$; Section <a href="#bl:sec:fields" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:sec:fields">7</a> returns the resulting sections and generated multiples to the original field. The independent curve-base method is developed in Section <a href="#nu2:sec:method" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="nu2:sec:method">8</a>.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Conventions and the complex formulation

All boundaries are effective with rational coefficients in $[0,1]$. An adjoint is an actual $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor, and $\sim_{\mathbb Q}$ denotes rational linear equivalence. A rational Cartier divisor is semiample if a positive Cartier multiple is generated by global sections; its Iitaka dimension is the dimension of the images of all sufficiently divisible nonempty complete systems, with value $-\infty$ if no such system exists. For a nef divisor $D$ on a projective $d$-fold, $$\nu(D)=\max\{j:D^jH^{d-j}>0\}$$ for an ample divisor $H$; these intersections may be computed on a smooth resolution. We use log discrepancies, so coefficient-one divisors have log discrepancy zero. Standard conventions for lc, klt, dlt and dlt adjunction follow <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Kollar2013](#ref-Kollar2013)].

The complex proof uses only individual Cartier witnesses, rather than preservation of $\mathbb Q$-factoriality under a field extension.

<a id="stmt-bl-prop-complex-supported"></a>
<div id="bl:prop:complex-supported" class="proposition">

**Proposition 5** (Complex supported assertion). *Let $V$ be normal and projective over $\mathbb C$, and let $C$ be an effective rational boundary. Suppose that $K_V$ and every component of $C$ are individually $\mathbb Q$-Cartier, that $(V,C)$ is lc, and that it is klt outside $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\lfloor C\rfloor$. If a nonzero effective Cartier divisor $G\sim q(K_V+C)$ has support in $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\lfloor C\rfloor$, for a sufficiently divisible positive integer $q$, and $\mathcal O_V(G)|_{G_{\mathrm{red}}}$ is semiample on the whole reduced scheme, then $\kappa(V,K_V+C)>0$.*

</div>

This proposition is proved in Sections <a href="#bl:sec:covers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:sec:covers">[bl:sec:covers]</a>–<a href="#bl:sec:lifting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:sec:lifting">5</a>. Its hypotheses follow from those of Theorem <a href="#bl:thm:supported" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:thm:supported">1</a> over $\mathbb C$, and its individual Cartier requirements are the finite witnesses used in the final field transfer.

<a id="bl:cov:section"></a>
### Moving frames, cyclic covers, and residues

<span id="bl:sec:covers" label="bl:sec:covers"></span>

The first step in Proposition <a href="#bl:prop:complex-supported" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:prop:complex-supported">5</a> is to embed the entire lifting obstruction into logarithmic residue cohomology. Moving to a frame bundle supplies compatible roots without choosing roots separately on open sets. The complete cyclic covers retain the character summand needed for an injective residue map, including the poles along the extra coefficient-one boundary.

<a id="bl:cov:initial-data"></a>
Work over $\mathbb C$, and put $A=K_V+C$ and $S=G_{\mathrm{red}}$. Replace $(q,G)$ by $(bq,bG)$, for a sufficiently divisible positive integer $b$, so that semiampleness on the entire reduced scheme $S$ gives fixed identifications $$\label{bl:cov:initial-data}
 L=\mathcal O_V(G)\simeq\mathcal O_V(qA),\qquad
 L|_S\simeq f^*J,\qquad
 f:S\longrightarrow T=\mathbb P^\ell,\quad J=\mathcal O_T(1).$$ Indeed, a globally generated power of the original restriction defines $f$ by its complete linear system; surjectivity onto $T$ is unnecessary. Further powers preserve this property. We also clear all Cartier indices and boundary denominators that occur below. Let $s\in H^0(V,L)$ be the section with divisor $G$, and write $$G=\sum_i d_iS_i,\qquad S=\sum_iS_i,\qquad C=S+H+C_0.$$ Here $d_i>0$, $H$ is the reduced sum of the remaining coefficient-one components, and $C_0$ has coefficients in $[0,1)$ and shares no component with $S+H$. Choose $r$ divisible by $q$ and all $d_i$, and put $a=r/q$. The singularity properties used in this section, and in the remainder of the supported-pair argument, are log canonicity and klt singularities away from $S+H$. A dlt pair has both properties. Apart from normality, the Cartier hypotheses used are that $K_V$ and the indicated boundary components are $\mathbb Q$-Cartier, together with the actual identifications in <a href="#bl:cov:initial-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:cov:initial-data">[bl:cov:initial-data]</a>.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### The moving frame and the first cover

Let $p:P=L^\times\to V$ and $B=J^\times\to T$ denote the bundles of nonzero vectors. The tautological frame $w$ trivializes $p^*L$ on $P$. For $\lambda\in\mathbb G_m$, let $\lambda$ multiply the vectors in both bundles by $\lambda^r$. All weights below are pullback weights: a tensor $u$ has weight $m$ if $\lambda^*u=\lambda^m u$. In particular $w$ has weight $r$. The fixed restriction isomorphism gives $$S_P:=p^{-1}(S)=S\times_TB\longrightarrow B,$$ an equivariant projective morphism.

There is an invariant canonical comparison $\omega_P\simeq p^*\omega_V$, understood reflexively on the normal spaces. On a bundle chart with nonzero fiber coordinate $t$, it sends a top form $\eta$ on $V$ to $p^*\eta\wedge dt/t$. Replacing $t$ by $ut$, with $u$ invertible on the base, adds $du/u$ to $dt/t$; its wedge with $\eta$ vanishes. Thus the comparison glues and is invariant under scaling. The same construction applies to divisible adjoint powers. Moreover, the pullback pair $(P,p^*C)$ is lc and is klt off $S_P+p^*H$: locally a log resolution is the product of a log resolution on $V$ with $\mathbb G_m$, with the same discrepancy coefficients.

The ratio $u=p^*s/w$ is a regular function on $P$. Form the whole finite algebra $\mathcal O_P[y]/(y^r-u)$ and let <a id="bl:cov:first-cover"></a>$$\label{bl:cov:first-cover}
 \pi:Z\longrightarrow P$$ be its normalization. This is the cyclic-cover construction of <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[EsnaultViehweg1992](#ref-EsnaultViehweg1992), Section 3.5], applied to the trivial line bundle; we retain its full-algebra convention. Throughout, normalization of a generically reduced algebra means normalization in its full total ring of fractions. Thus $Z$ is a disjoint union of normal varieties if the generic algebra splits; no component is discarded. The polynomial is separable over every generic field, and normalization is finite since the schemes are of finite type over $\mathbb C$. The function $y$ remains regular, and the action lifts by $$\lambda^*y=\lambda^{-1}y.$$ It commutes with the deck action $y\mapsto\zeta y$, $\zeta\in\mu_r$.

<a id="stmt-bl-cov-reduced-cover"></a>
<div id="bl:cov:reduced-cover" class="lemma">

<a id="bl:cov:crepant"></a>
**Lemma 6**. *The divisor $E=(y=0)$ on $Z$ is reduced and Cartier, with ideal $I=(y)=\mathcal O_Z(-E)$. Put $H_Z=\pi^*p^*H$ and $C_{0,Z}=\pi^*p^*C_0$. With compatible canonical divisors one has $$\label{bl:cov:crepant}
 K_Z+E+H_Z+C_{0,Z}=\pi^*(K_P+p^*C).$$ The pair $(Z,E+H_Z+C_{0,Z})$ is lc and is klt off $E+H_Z$. The map $E\to S_P$ induces an equivariant projective morphism $g:E\to B$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-bl-cov-reduced-cover"><summary>Proof of stmt-bl-cov-reduced-cover</summary>

*Proof.* At the generic point of $p^{-1}(S_i)$, write $u=vx^{d_i}$ in the corresponding discrete valuation ring, with $v$ a unit. After an unramified extension extracting a root of $v$, the normalized Kummer algebra has ramification index $e_i=r/d_i$ on each branch, since $d_i$ divides $r$. Consequently $$\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_F(y)=e_id_i/r=1$$ at every prime $F$ lying over this divisor. These are all the zero divisors of $y$. On every normal component of $Z$, the nonzero function $y$ is a nonzerodivisor and its principal ideal is radical. For the latter assertion, if $v^N\in(y)$, the height-one valuation inequalities give $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_F(v)\geq\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_F(y)$ at every prime; normality then implies $v/y\in\mathcal O_Z$. This also proves that $E$ is reduced as a scheme.

Off $S_P$ the first root algebra is finite étale. At a prime above $S_P$ the canonical ramification coefficient is $e_i-1$, whereas the pullback coefficient of $S_P$ is $e_i$. This proves <a href="#bl:cov:crepant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:cov:crepant">[bl:cov:crepant]</a> in codimension one, and hence as an identity of $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisors. In particular $H_Z$ is reduced and the coefficients of $C_{0,Z}$ are those of $C_0$.

We record why this calculation controls all divisorial valuations, including exceptional ones. For a finite dominant map of normal varieties $X'\to X$ in characteristic zero and a comparison $K_{X'}+\Delta'=\phi^*(K_X+\Delta)$, let $v'$ be a normalized divisorial valuation upstairs. Its restriction is $e v$ for a normalized divisorial valuation $v$ downstairs: the residue extension is finite, so its transcendence degree is still $\dim X-1$. Choose a model of $X$ on which $v$ is a divisor and normalize it in the upstairs function field. At the resulting discrete valuation rings the different has order $e-1$ (the extension is tame). Comparing the canonical coefficients on these models yields <a id="bl:cov:finite-discrepancy"></a>$$\label{bl:cov:finite-discrepancy}
 A_{X',\Delta'}(v')=e A_{X,\Delta}(v),$$ where $A$ denotes log discrepancy. The same argument applies to each field factor of a disconnected cover. Applied to <a href="#bl:cov:crepant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:cov:crepant">[bl:cov:crepant]</a>, it gives lc singularities everywhere and klt singularities over $P\setminus(S_P+p^*H)$. Finally, the reduced scheme $E$ maps into $S_P$ because its image is supported there. The resulting map is finite, so its composite with $S_P\to B$ is projective. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### The root of the adjoint tensor

<a id="bl:cov:tau"></a>
The nowhere-vanishing frame $w$, the fixed isomorphism in <a href="#bl:cov:initial-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:cov:initial-data">[bl:cov:initial-data]</a>, and the canonical comparison produce a meromorphic $q$-canonical tensor on $P$. As an adjoint section it has no zeros, so its divisor as a meromorphic tensor is $-qp^*C$. Its differential pullback to $Z$ is a tensor $\tau$, nonzero on every component, satisfying $$\label{bl:cov:tau}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Div}}(\tau)=-q\Delta_Z,\qquad
 \Delta_Z=E+H_Z+C_{0,Z},\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{wt}}(\tau)=r.$$ The canonical ramification formula used in <a href="#bl:cov:crepant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:cov:crepant">[bl:cov:crepant]</a> proves this equality, including its integral coefficients. Thus the construction depends on the given rational linear equivalence, not merely on a numerical equivalence.

<a id="bl:cov:second-algebra"></a>
Let $\nu:\widetilde Z\to Z$ be the full normalized $q$-th root of $\tau$. Explicitly, for a meromorphic canonical frame $\theta$ on a component with function field $K$, normalize in the finite reduced $K$-algebra $$\label{bl:cov:second-algebra}
 K[c]/\bigl(c^q-\tau/\theta^{\otimes q}\bigr).$$ Changing $\theta$ to $v\theta$ changes $c$ to $c/v$, so the algebras and the tautological form $c\theta$ glue. Pullbacks of forms in this description are differential pullbacks. The deck group $\mu_q$ acts by $c\mapsto\zeta c$, and the tautological form has its character $\chi(\zeta)=\zeta$. Even if the algebra splits, its invariant subalgebra is exactly $K$: the basis $1,c,\ldots,c^{q-1}$ has all the distinct characters. This observation will be needed for descent of ratios of forms.

The $\mathbb G_m$-action also lifts algebraically. If on a meromorphic chart $\lambda^*\theta=j_\lambda\theta$, using differentials relative to the parameter $\lambda$, prescribe <a id="bl:cov:second-action"></a>$$\label{bl:cov:second-action}
 \lambda^*c=\lambda^a j_\lambda^{-1}c.$$ This respects the equation because $aq=r$ and $\lambda^*\tau=\lambda^r\tau$; the factors $j_\lambda$ satisfy the cocycle identity. These formulas define the action on the generic algebra and on its integral closure. More explicitly, normalization commutes with the smooth base extension by $\mathbb G_m$, and the isomorphism between the two pullback algebras over $\mathbb G_m\times Z$ therefore induces an isomorphism of their normalizations. This gives a regular action, not just a separate lift of each scalar automorphism. It commutes with $\mu_q$, and the tautological form has weight $a$.
