Take a projective $(\mathbb G_m\times\mu_q)$-equivariant log resolution of $\widetilde Z$ and the inverse images of the boundary supports, and write its composite with $\nu$ as $$\rho:Y\longrightarrow Z.$$ Functorial resolution and principalization in characteristic zero give this resolution by invariant centers and projective blowups <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[BierstoneMilman2008](#ref-BierstoneMilman2008), Theorem 1.1], <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Wlodarczyk2005](#ref-Wlodarczyk2005), Theorem 1.0.1]. Indeed, functoriality for the smooth action and projection morphisms $(\mathbb G_m\times\mu_q)\times\widetilde Z\to\widetilde Z$ lifts the action and its group identities at every step. We use the pulled-back tautological top form $\sigma$ on $Y$; it has deck character $\chi$ and weight $a$. We may choose $Y$ smooth and quasi-projective with a $\mathbb G_m$-linearized ample line bundle. To see the latter point, compactify $P$ as $\mathbb P_V(\mathcal O_V\oplus L)$, with its scaling action. A sufficiently positive twist of the tautological bundle by an ample bundle from $V$ is ample and linearized. Restrict to $P$ and pull back through the finite covers, which preserves ampleness and linearization. For every invariant blowup center its ideal, and hence the tautological relatively ample bundle on the blowup, is linearized. Tensoring these with sufficiently high powers of the preceding ample bundles at successive steps gives the assertion for $Y$.

Define effective divisors on $Y$ by <a id="bl:cov:resolved-boundary"></a>$$\label{bl:cov:resolved-boundary}
 \widehat E=\rho^*E,\qquad
 D_h=(\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\rho^*H_Z)_{\mathrm{red}},\qquad
 R=\sum_{\substack{F\text{ prime in }\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\widehat E\\
                         F\not\subset D_h}} F.$$ The union $R+D_h$ is reduced simple normal crossing. Both $R$ and $\widehat E$ map scheme-theoretically to $E$: the function $y$ vanishes on them. Write $q_R:R\to E$ and $q_{\widehat E}:\widehat E\to E$ for these projective maps, and set $h=g\circ q_R:R\to B$.

The spaces and morphisms are summarized in Figure <a href="#fig:boundary-covers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:boundary-covers">1</a>. The form $\sigma$ lives on $Y$; its residue will live on $R$ and will be pushed through the top row to $B$.

<figure id="fig:boundary-covers">

<figcaption>The reduced boundary maps to $B$ through the top row, whose composite is $h$. The lower row contains the first normalized root cover $π$ and the second normalized cover followed by resolution, combined in $ρ$. Only the boundary is equipped with a map to $B$.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-boundary-covers"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:boundary-covers`)


<a id="stmt-bl-cov-poles"></a>
<div id="bl:cov:poles" class="lemma">

<a id="bl:cov:residue-form"></a>
**Lemma 7**. *The tautological form and its residue satisfy $$\label{bl:cov:residue-form}
 \sigma\in H^0\bigl(Y,\omega_Y(R+D_h)\bigr),\qquad
 \Omega=\mathop{\mathrm{res}}_R(\sigma)\in H^0\bigl(R,\omega_R(D_h)\bigr).$$ Here $\omega_R$ is the absolute dualizing sheaf of the reduced simple-normal-crossing divisor $R$. Both forms have weight $a$ and deck character $\chi$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-bl-cov-poles"><summary>Proof of stmt-bl-cov-poles</summary>

<a id="bl:cov:sigma-order"></a>
*Proof.* Let $F$ be any prime divisor on $Y$, and restrict its valuation to a function-field component of $Z$, obtaining $e v$. On a downstairs model carrying $v$, <a href="#bl:cov:tau" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:cov:tau">[bl:cov:tau]</a> gives order $q(A_{Z,\Delta_Z}(v)-1)$ for $\tau$. Differential pullback adds $q(e-1)$ to $e$ times this order. Since $\sigma^{\otimes q}=\rho^*\tau$, $$\label{bl:cov:sigma-order}
 \mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_F(\sigma)+1=e A_{Z,\Delta_Z}(v).$$ Log canonicity makes the right side nonnegative. If the center lies outside $E+H_Z$, it is strictly positive by the klt property. The order on the left is integral, so $\sigma$ has at most a simple pole, and every pole is over $E+H_Z$. Because $E$ and $H_Z$ are effective $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisors, a prime whose center lies in this union occurs in the support of their pullback. That reduced support is exactly $R+D_h$. This proves the first assertion. Adjunction for the effective Cartier divisor $R$ on the smooth variety $Y$ gives the residue sequence <a id="bl:cov:residue-sequence"></a>$$\label{bl:cov:residue-sequence}
 0\longrightarrow\omega_Y(D_h)
 \longrightarrow\omega_Y(R+D_h)
 \longrightarrow\omega_R(D_h)\longrightarrow0.$$ It gives $\Omega$ and is equivariant, so residue preserves the stated weight and character. ◻

</details>

The definition of $D_h$ is essential here. An exceptional divisor over $E\cap H_Z$ belongs to $D_h$ and is omitted from $R$, but its possible simple pole is still allowed in <a href="#bl:cov:residue-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:cov:residue-form">[bl:cov:residue-form]</a>. No disjointness condition on $H$ and the strata of $S$ has been used.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### The residue summand

<a id="stmt-bl-lem-residue-injection"></a>
<div id="bl:lem:residue-injection" class="lemma">

<a id="bl:cov:derived-split"></a>
<a id="bl:cov:injections"></a>
**Lemma 8** (Residue injection). *Multiplication by $\Omega$ after pullback induces a split morphism $$\label{bl:cov:derived-split}
 \mathcal O_E\longrightarrow \mathbf R(q_R)_*\omega_R(D_h)$$ in the derived category of $\mathcal O_E$-modules. Consequently, for every $i\geq0$, it induces an injection $$\label{bl:cov:injections}
 R^ig_*\mathcal O_E\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow
 R^ih_*\omega_R(D_h),\qquad e'\longmapsto\Omega e'.$$ In particular this holds for $i=1$. The map increases pullback weight by $a$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-bl-lem-residue-injection"><summary>Proof of stmt-bl-lem-residue-injection</summary>

<a id="bl:cov:eigensheaf"></a>
*Proof.* *The character summand.* We first identify the $\chi$-eigensheaf: $$\label{bl:cov:eigensheaf}
 \bigl(\rho_*\omega_Y(D_h)\bigr)_\chi
   =\sigma\cdot\mathcal O_Z(-E).$$ A meromorphic $\chi$-form divided by $\sigma$ is deck-invariant, so it is the pullback of a meromorphic function $v$ on $Z$, by the invariant algebra calculation following <a href="#bl:cov:second-algebra" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:cov:second-algebra">[bl:cov:second-algebra]</a>. At a prime of $Z$ with coefficient $d$ in $\Delta_Z$, and at a prime of the cover above its generic point, <a href="#bl:cov:sigma-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:cov:sigma-order">[bl:cov:sigma-order]</a> becomes $$\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_F(v\sigma)=e\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}(v)+e(1-d)-1.$$ Over $E$ no pole is permitted in $\omega_Y(D_h)$, so $d=1$ forces $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}(v)\geq1$. Over $H_Z$ a simple pole is permitted and forces $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}(v)\geq0$. Elsewhere $0\leq d<1$, and the integer $e(1-d)$ lies between $1$ and $e$; hence holomorphy is equivalent to $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}(v)\geq0$. These necessary inequalities, and normality, give $v\in\mathcal O_Z(-E)$. Conversely such a function pulls back to a regular function vanishing along $\widehat E$. Since $\widehat E\geq R$, it cancels every pole of $\sigma$ outside $D_h$ on the whole resolution, including exceptional divisors. This proves <a href="#bl:cov:eigensheaf" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:cov:eigensheaf">[bl:cov:eigensheaf]</a>.

<a id="bl:cov:vanishing"></a>
*Vanishing and the thickened residue.* We next prove the required vanishing: $$\label{bl:cov:vanishing}
 R^j\rho_*\omega_Y(D_h)=0\qquad(j>0).$$ Choose a rational $\epsilon>0$ so small that every nonzero coefficient of $F=\epsilon\rho^*H_Z$ lies strictly between $0$ and $1$. Then $D_h-F$ is an effective simple-normal-crossing boundary with all coefficients less than one, and $$(K_Y+D_h)-\bigl(K_Y+(D_h-F)\bigr)=F.$$ The divisor $F$ is $\rho$-nef, being a pullback from $Z$, and $\rho$-big: $\rho$ is projective and generically finite on every component, and every divisor restricts to a big divisor on its zero-dimensional generic fiber. Relative Kawamata–Viehweg vanishing therefore applies to the Cartier divisor $K_Y+D_h$ and the klt boundary $D_h-F$; see <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[FujinoVanishing](#ref-FujinoVanishing), Theorem 3.3]. If $H=0$, take $F=D_h=0$; the same relative bigness observation includes this case. This proves <a href="#bl:cov:vanishing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:cov:vanishing">[bl:cov:vanishing]</a> without asserting any absolute nefness of an auxiliary adjoint.

<a id="bl:cov:two-derived-images"></a>
Since $E$ is Cartier, the projection formula gives the same vanishing for $\omega_Y(\widehat E+D_h)$ and, by <a href="#bl:cov:eigensheaf" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:cov:eigensheaf">[bl:cov:eigensheaf]</a>, identifies their derived eigensummands as $$\label{bl:cov:two-derived-images}
 \begin{split}
 \bigl(\mathbf R\rho_*\omega_Y(D_h)\bigr)_\chi
     &\simeq\mathcal O_Z(-E),\\
 \bigl(\mathbf R\rho_*\omega_Y(\widehat E+D_h)\bigr)_\chi
     &\simeq\mathcal O_Z.
 \end{split}$$ Both are concentrated in degree zero; the isomorphisms are multiplication by $\sigma$. The inclusion of the two sheaves on $Y$ corresponds to the ordinary ideal inclusion $\mathcal O_Z(-E)\hookrightarrow
\mathcal O_Z$.

<a id="bl:cov:thick-summand"></a>
For the possibly nonreduced effective Cartier divisor $\widehat E$, adjunction still gives $$\omega_{\widehat E}(D_h)
   =\omega_Y(\widehat E+D_h)/\omega_Y(D_h).$$ Taking the quotient in <a href="#bl:cov:two-derived-images" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:cov:two-derived-images">[bl:cov:two-derived-images]</a> shows that $$\label{bl:cov:thick-summand}
 \bigl(\mathbf R(q_{\widehat E})_*
                 \omega_{\widehat E}(D_h)\bigr)_\chi
       \simeq\mathcal O_E$$ in the derived category on $E$. Here this conclusion can be checked on cohomology sheaves after the exact faithful pushforward by the closed immersion $E\hookrightarrow Z$: the higher eigencohomology vanishes and the degree-zero map is the residue of multiplication by $\sigma$. There is no need to assert full faithfulness of derived pushforward for that immersion.

*Retraction from the reduced residue.* We construct the splitting map on $E$ itself. Pullback of functions and multiplication by $\Omega$ give $$\alpha:\mathcal O_E\longrightarrow\mathbf R(q_R)_*\omega_R(D_h).$$ The inequality $R\leq\widehat E$ gives an inclusion of residue quotients on $Y$, hence a morphism $$\beta:\mathbf R(q_R)_*\omega_R(D_h)
       \longrightarrow\mathbf R(q_{\widehat E})_*
                                      \omega_{\widehat E}(D_h).$$ These are $\mathcal O_E$-linear: multiplication by the pulled-back equation $y$ kills both quotients. If a local function on $E$ is lifted to $v\in\mathcal O_Z$, the composite $\beta\alpha$ is represented by the class of $\rho^*v\,\sigma$ modulo $\omega_Y(D_h)$. Changing $v$ by a multiple of $y$ does not change this class, since $y\sigma\in\omega_Y(D_h)$ by $\widehat E\geq R$. Thus projection onto the $\chi$-summand followed by <a href="#bl:cov:thick-summand" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:cov:thick-summand">[bl:cov:thick-summand]</a> sends $\beta\alpha$ to the identity. The projection is available in the derived category because averaging over $\mu_q$ gives the idempotent $q^{-1}\sum_{\zeta\in\mu_q}\chi(\zeta)^{-1}\zeta^*$. This supplies a retraction of $\alpha$ and proves <a href="#bl:cov:derived-split" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:cov:derived-split">[bl:cov:derived-split]</a>. Finally apply $\mathbf Rg_*$ and take cohomology to obtain <a href="#bl:cov:injections" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:cov:injections">[bl:cov:injections]</a>. The weight statement follows from $\mathop{\mathrm{wt}}(\Omega)=a$. ◻

</details>

<a id="bl:cov:obstruction-contract"></a>
#### The obstruction to be detected

Put $A_d=|g|_*(\mathcal O_Z/I^d)$ for $d\geq1$, pushing the underlying sheaves of complex vector spaces in the Zariski topology along $|g|:|E|\to|B|$. This uses no morphism from a thickening to $B$. Fix $k\geq1$ and suppose that $A_{d+1}\to A_d$ is surjective for $1\leq d<k$. The current connecting map is $\partial_k:A_k\to R^1g_*(I^k/I^{k+1})$. Section <a href="#bl:sec:lifting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:sec:lifting">5</a> will show that it factors, after framing $I^k/I^{k+1}$ by $y^k$, through a derivation $$\delta_k:g_*\mathcal O_E\longrightarrow R^1g_*\mathcal O_E,
 \qquad \partial_k(\widetilde F)=y^k\delta_k(F).$$ Here $\widetilde F\in A_k$ is any local lift of $F\in g_*\mathcal O_E$. The factorization and derivation rule will be proved there from the previous surjections. Since $y$ has weight $-1$, this derivation has degree $k$ for the frame action.

Restrict $\delta_k$ to pullbacks of functions on $B$. In local coordinates $t_i$, the residue injection produces a global section of $R^1h_*\omega_R(D_h)\otimes T_B$, given by $$v_k=\sum_i\Omega\delta_k(g^*t_i)\otimes\partial_{t_i}
 ,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{wt}}(v_k)=a+k>0.$$ The derivation rule makes this tensor independent of coordinates. The next two sections develop a differential-operator test for it. The graph identity that makes the test applicable, and that then annihilates $\delta_k$ on all of $g_*\mathcal O_E$, is proved with the induction in Section <a href="#bl:sec:lifting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:sec:lifting">5</a>.

<a id="bl:sec:hodge"></a>
### A filtered module on the boundary graph

The tensor $v_k$ of Section <a href="#bl:cov:obstruction-contract" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:cov:obstruction-contract">2.4</a> has coefficients in $R^1h_*\omega_R(D_h)$. We construct a filtered right $\mathcal D_B$-module whose lowest piece is this entire coherent sheaf, so that differentiation in base coordinates gives a first symbol for the tensor. We then prove a vanishing statement for the kernel of that symbol on a projective base. Section <a href="#bl:sec:frames" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:sec:frames">4</a> will apply it to the positive frame weight $a+k$.

Temporarily isolate the graph calculation from the covers. Let $Y$ be a smooth complex quasi-projective variety, let $R+D_h$ be a reduced simple normal crossing divisor with no common component between $R$ and $D_h$, and let $h:R\to B$ be a projective morphism to a smooth quasi-projective variety. Write $b=\dim B$, and let $Q\subset Y\times B$ be its graph. The projection is not required to be proper, but its restriction to $Q$ is projective: $$\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
R \arrow[r,hook,"{(\iota,h)}"] \arrow[dr,"h"'] &
Y\times B \arrow[d,"\operatorname{pr}_B"]\\
& B.
\end{tikzcd}$$ Throughout this section $D_h$ also denotes its pullback to the product.

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
#### Conventions and the lowest filtration piece

We use right $\mathcal D$-modules underlying graded-polarizable algebraic mixed Hodge modules with rational structures. On a smooth $d$-fold we twist the constant Hodge module by $d$, so that its underlying canonical sheaf starts at $F_0$, rather than $F_{-d}$. All subsequent localization and support functors carry this normalization. The required functorial statements are the localization and support operations, exterior products, smooth inverse images, and projective direct-image strictness in <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Saito1988](#ref-Saito1988), [Saito1990](#ref-Saito1990), [Saito2013](#ref-Saito2013)]; conventions are also explained in <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Schnell2014](#ref-Schnell2014), [Popa2016](#ref-Popa2016)].

<a id="bl:hod:modules"></a>
Define $$\label{bl:hod:modules}
 \mathcal M=\mathcal H^{b+1}_{[Q]}
       \bigl(\omega_{Y\times B}(*D_h)\bigr),
 \qquad
 \mathcal N=\mathcal H^1\operatorname{pr}_{B,+}\mathcal M.$$ Here the brackets denote algebraic local cohomology, and the plus sign denotes differential-module direct image. Localization and cohomology with supports show that $\mathcal M$ is the underlying filtered module of a mixed Hodge module. Its direct image has projective support, as above.

<a id="bl:hod:relative-dr"></a>
For a right module, the relative filtered de Rham complex for the projection has term $$\label{bl:hod:relative-dr}
 F_{j-i}\mathcal M\otimes\bigwedge^i T_Y
 \quad\text{in degree }-i.$$ The tangent sheaf here is pulled back from $Y$. In particular its last term, in degree zero, is $F_j\mathcal M$.

<a id="stmt-bl-lem-lowest-piece"></a>
<div id="bl:lem:lowest-piece" class="lemma">

**Lemma 9** (Lowest piece and strict insertion). *With the normalization above, <a id="bl:hod:lowest-source"></a><a id="bl:hod:lowest-target"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 F_{<0}\mathcal M&=0,&
 F_0\mathcal M&=(\iota,h)_*\omega_R(D_h),
 \label{bl:hod:lowest-source}\\
 F_{<0}\mathcal N&=0,&
 F_0\mathcal N&=R^1h_*\omega_R(D_h).
 \label{bl:hod:lowest-target}
\end{aligned}$$ The equality for $F_0\mathcal N$ identifies it with a subsheaf of $\mathcal N$. It is induced by simple graph residues and the inclusion of the last term of the relative de Rham complex. The latter inclusion commutes with right differentiation in base coordinates.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-bl-lem-lowest-piece"><summary>Proof of stmt-bl-lem-lowest-piece</summary>

*Proof.* Work locally along $Q$. Take simple normal crossing coordinates for $R+D_h$, write $r_0$ for the reduced equation of $R$, and take coordinates $t_1,\ldots,t_b$ on the base. The functions $h^*t_i$ on $R$ lift locally to functions $v_i$ on $Y$. Thus $Q$ is defined by $$r_0=0,\qquad \psi_i=t_i-v_i=0\quad(1\leq i\leq b).$$ The $\psi_i$, together with the boundary coordinates, are independent. The support sequence is a hypersurface followed by $b$ smooth support coordinates, which explains the index $b+1$ in <a href="#bl:hod:modules" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:hod:modules">[bl:hod:modules]</a>.

<a id="bl:hod:boundary-module"></a>
Before adding the graph coordinates, the boundary module is $$\label{bl:hod:boundary-module}
 \omega_Y(*(R+D_h))/\omega_Y(*D_h).$$ In one coordinate, localization of the normalized constant begins with simple poles at $F_0$; $F_j$ permits $j$ further derivatives. The quotient by regular forms is the residue delta module, whose simple residue is again at level zero. This is the usual localization–residue exact sequence with its strict Hodge filtration. Taking exterior products gives the same rule for a normal crossing divisor: one begins with simple poles in all the indicated coordinates, and allows a total of at most $j$ extra denominator exponents; see also <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[MustataPopa2019](#ref-MustataPopa2019), Section 3 and Proposition 8.2]. Quotienting out the part regular in the $R$-support direction gives <a href="#bl:hod:boundary-module" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:hod:boundary-module">[bl:hod:boundary-module]</a>. The $b$ independent graph residues add free normal derivatives with the same order rule.

<a id="bl:hod:simple-graph"></a>
It follows that $F_{<0}\mathcal M=0$, and its lowest piece is $$\omega_Y(R+D_h)/\omega_Y(D_h)=\omega_R(D_h),$$ inserted along the graph by the class $$\label{bl:hod:simple-graph}
 \alpha\longmapsto
 \left[\alpha\,
   \frac{dt_1\wedge\cdots\wedge dt_b}
        {\prod_{i=1}^b(t_i-v_i)}\right],
 \qquad \alpha\in\omega_Y(R+D_h).$$ A form regular in the $R$-support direction gives zero, even though poles on $D_h$ are allowed. This computes the absolute dualizing sheaf of the whole reduced divisor, including its crossings.

The map is independent of the lifts. If $v_i$ changes by a multiple of $r_0$, the correction to the simple graph fraction becomes regular in the support direction and vanishes. Under a base coordinate change the Jacobian in the top differential cancels the determinant in the ordered graph residues. These are also the usual transformation rules for a complete-intersection residue. Thus <a href="#bl:hod:simple-graph" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:hod:simple-graph">[bl:hod:simple-graph]</a> is intrinsic.

For completeness, strictness applies here to a projective morphism, despite the quasi-projective ambient notation. Compactify the projection projectively over $B$. Since $Q\to B$ is proper, its image in that compactification remains closed and acquires no points in the relative boundary. Extend the object with this unchanged support. Projective filtered strictness applies to it and hence computes the original direct image. The mixed statement is Saito’s Theorem 2.14 and Proposition 2.15 in <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Saito1990](#ref-Saito1990)], with the algebraic formulation in its Section 4.1; no decomposition of a mixed object is needed.

At level zero, <a href="#bl:hod:relative-dr" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:hod:relative-dr">[bl:hod:relative-dr]</a> has only the last term. Its first cohomological direct image is therefore $R^1h_*\omega_R(D_h)$. Strictness identifies this with $F_0\mathcal N$ as a subsheaf of $\mathcal N$, and gives the assertion at negative levels as well. Finally, base differentiation commutes with the relative differential in the product. It therefore commutes with the map from the last term to differential-module direct image. ◻

</details>

The same calculation describes every filtration level locally: $F_j\mathcal M$ consists of the graph fractions with simple initial denominators in the boundary and graph coordinates, and at most $j$ extra denominator exponents in total, modulo the fractions regular in the $R$-support direction. We will use this explicit description across a ramified base change.

<a id="pm-h-15"></a>
#### The consequence of Kodaira–Saito vanishing

<a id="bl:hod:ksv"></a>
For a mixed Hodge module $\mathcal N_0$ on a smooth projective variety $T_0$ and an ample line bundle $H_0$, Kodaira–Saito vanishing gives $$\label{bl:hod:ksv}
 \mathbb H^i\!\left(T_0,
     \mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_j^F\mathop{\mathrm{DR}}(\mathcal N_0)\otimes H_0^{-1}\right)=0
 \qquad(i<0).$$ We use perverse normalization, so the right de Rham complex ends in degree zero. The mixed version follows from the pure strict-support decomposition and the strict finite weight filtration; see <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Saito1990](#ref-Saito1990), Proposition 2.33] and <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Popa2016](#ref-Popa2016), Theorem 28]. Tate twists do not affect the assertion, which holds for every $j$.

<a id="stmt-bl-lem-symbol-vanishing"></a>
<div id="bl:lem:symbol-vanishing" class="lemma">

**Lemma 10** (No ample line in the lowest symbol kernel). *Suppose $F_{<0}\mathcal N_0=0$. Then <a id="bl:hod:symbol-vanishing"></a>$$\label{bl:hod:symbol-vanishing}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\!\left(H_0,
  \ker\left[F_0\mathcal N_0\otimes T_{T_0}
                \longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_1^F\mathcal N_0\right]\right)=0.$$ The arrow is the symbol of the right differential-operator action.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-bl-lem-symbol-vanishing"><summary>Proof of stmt-bl-lem-symbol-vanishing</summary>

*Proof.* At $j=1$ all terms in degrees at most $-2$ vanish, so the graded de Rham complex is the two-term complex $$[F_0\mathcal N_0\otimes T_{T_0}\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_1^F\mathcal N_0]
 \quad\text{in degrees }-1,0.$$ Its hypercohomology in degree $-1$, after tensoring by $H_0^{-1}$, is exactly the space of global sections of the twisted kernel. Equation <a href="#bl:hod:ksv" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:hod:ksv">[bl:hod:ksv]</a> proves the claim. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-bl-hod-whole-sheaf"></a>
<div id="bl:hod:whole-sheaf" class="remark">

*Remark 11*. Both Lemma <a href="#bl:lem:lowest-piece" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:lem:lowest-piece">9</a> and Lemma <a href="#bl:lem:symbol-vanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:lem:symbol-vanishing">10</a> concern complete coherent sheaves, including sections on smaller supports. They require no local freeness and no restriction to the open set carrying a variation of Hodge structure. This is essential for the obstruction calculation.

</div>

<a id="bl:sec:frames"></a>
### Positive frame weight and the Euler direction

We now combine the graph module with the scaling action on the frame bundle. A positive weight has two consequences: after a transverse cover it gives an ample source for the horizontal symbol, and in the vertical direction it gives a nonzero Euler eigenvalue. The proof keeps these two operations separate until both have been identified on the same right differential module.

Return to Section <a href="#bl:sec:covers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:sec:covers">[bl:sec:covers]</a>. Thus $T=\mathbb P^\ell$, $J=\mathcal O_T(1)$, and $B=J^\times$ is the bundle of nonzero vectors, with projection $p_B:B\to T$. The parameter $\lambda\in\mathbb G_m$ acts on $B$ by multiplication by $\lambda^r$. Its action on $Y$ preserves $R$ and $D_h$, and $h:R\to B$ is equivariant. All the modules in <a href="#bl:hod:modules" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:hod:modules">[bl:hod:modules]</a> inherit their natural geometric action.

<a id="bl:frm:euler"></a>
Let $\varepsilon$ be the infinitesimal generator on $B$. In a bundle chart with fiber coordinate $\beta$, $$\label{bl:frm:euler}
 \varepsilon=r\beta\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta}.$$ It is nowhere zero and spans the kernel of $T_B\to p_B^*T_T$. By weight $m$ we mean pullback weight: $\lambda^*s=\lambda^m s$.

<a id="stmt-bl-prop-frame-positivity"></a>
<div id="bl:prop:frame-positivity" class="proposition">

**Proposition 12** (Positive-weight obstruction). *For the filtered module $\mathcal N$ of <a href="#bl:hod:modules" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:hod:modules">[bl:hod:modules]</a>, the following assertions hold.*

1.  *If $v\in H^0(B,F_0\mathcal N\otimes T_B)$ is homogeneous of weight $m>0$ and has zero symbol in $\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_1^F\mathcal N$, then its image in $F_0\mathcal N\otimes p_B^*T_T$ is zero.*

<a id="bl:frm:euler-action"></a>
2.  *If $s\in H^0(B,F_0\mathcal N)$ is homogeneous of weight $m$, then its actual right differential-operator action satisfies $$\label{bl:frm:euler-action}
     s\varepsilon=-ms.$$ The same assertion is valid on invariant bundle charts.*

</div>

The first assertion concerns the symbol, and the second concerns the operator itself. Their combination will annihilate the obstruction. We prove them separately, keeping track of both canonical factors and filtration shifts.

The horizontal directions cannot in general be suppressed. For example, if $V=\mathbb P^2$ and $C$ is a smooth quartic, then $A=K_V+C\sim\mathcal O_{\mathbb P^2}(1)$ and $G=C\sim4A$ satisfy the supported hypotheses, while $\mathcal O_V(G)|_C$ is ample and nontrivial. The construction must therefore retain the full map $f:S\to T$.

<a id="pm-h-17"></a>
#### A transverse power cover

<a id="bl:frm:bundle-square"></a>
Suppose first that $\ell>0$. Take the coordinate $r$-th power map of $\mathbb P^\ell$ and compose it with a general target automorphism, obtaining $$\phi:T'=\mathbb P^\ell\longrightarrow T,
 \qquad J'=\mathcal O_{T'}(1),\qquad (J')^r\simeq\phi^*J.$$ It is finite flat. Put $B'=(J')^\times$, with its ordinary scalar action, and let $p':B'\to T'$ be the projection. Raising frames to the $r$-th power gives a finite flat equivariant map $\eta$ in $$\label{bl:frm:bundle-square}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
B'=(J')^\times \arrow[r,"\eta"] \arrow[d,"p'"'] &
B=J^\times \arrow[d,"p_B"]\\
T' \arrow[r,"\phi"'] & T.
\end{tikzcd}$$ Along each nonzero fiber its differential is invertible.

Choose $\phi$ transverse to the maps to $T$ from every closed intersection of components of $R+D_h$ involving a component of $R$. These intersections are smooth and there are finitely many of them. Here is the generic-transversality justification. For such an intersection $L$, the matching-pair space for the map $L\to T$ and the translated power map is smooth: variation of the target automorphism supplies every target tangent direction. A general fiber over the automorphism group is smooth by generic smoothness in characteristic zero. This is exactly the required transversality. The finitely many conditions can be imposed simultaneously. Since the vertical differential in <a href="#bl:frm:bundle-square" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:frm:bundle-square">[bl:frm:bundle-square]</a> is invertible, this also gives transversality to the corresponding maps $L\to B$. If a point of $T$ is prescribed, one may in addition require that the branch divisor avoid it, an open condition on the target automorphism.

Let $Q'=Q\times_B B'\subset Y\times B'$, and set <a id="bl:frm:basechanged-modules"></a>$$\label{bl:frm:basechanged-modules}
 \mathcal M'=\mathcal H^{b+1}_{[Q']}
     \bigl(\omega_{Y\times B'}(*D_h)\bigr),
 \qquad
 \mathcal N'=\mathcal H^1\operatorname{pr}_{B',+}\mathcal M'.$$ These are again mixed Hodge modules with projective support.

<a id="stmt-bl-frm-comparison"></a>
<div id="bl:frm:comparison" class="lemma">

<a id="bl:frm:filtered-comparison"></a>
**Lemma 13** (Filtered transverse comparison). *For every $j$ there are natural equivariant isomorphisms $$\label{bl:frm:filtered-comparison}
 F_j\mathcal N'
   \simeq \eta^*F_j\mathcal N\otimes\omega_{B'/B}.$$ Under these isomorphisms, symbol action transforms by the tangent map $d\eta:T_{B'}\to\eta^*T_B$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-bl-frm-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-bl-frm-comparison</summary>

*Proof.* Use the local coordinates in the proof of Lemma <a href="#bl:lem:lowest-piece" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:lem:lowest-piece">9</a>. The pulled-back graph equations are $$\psi'_i=t_i\circ\eta-v_i.$$ At a point of $Q'$, transversality to the intersection of all boundary components through that point says that the differentials of the $\psi'_i$, together with those boundary coordinates, are independent. They can therefore be used as part of a local smooth coordinate system. In particular the local support calculation remains a normal crossing calculation.

Flat pullback preserves the localization quotient and its local cohomology. More specifically, the explicit description following Lemma <a href="#bl:lem:lowest-piece" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:lem:lowest-piece">9</a> identifies its filtration level by level: the fractions with total extra denominator exponent at most $j$ pull back to precisely the corresponding fractions for $\mathcal M'$. For right modules one must also replace the absolute top-form frame on $Y\times B$ by that on $Y\times B'$. Thus the filtered comparison before direct image is $$F_j\mathcal M'
  \simeq (\mathop{\mathrm{id}}_Y\times\eta)^*F_j\mathcal M
                \otimes\operatorname{pr}_{B'}^*\omega_{B'/B}.$$ This is an isomorphism with the abstract relative canonical line; it does not multiply sections by a Jacobian that vanishes at ramification.

The relative de Rham differentials differentiate only in $Y$. They commute with this comparison and with its relative canonical factor. Flat base change therefore applies to the entire filtered relative de Rham complex. One can check it on a finite affine cover and its Čech complex; flat pullback commutes with all cohomology in question. Projective-support strictness identifies the cohomology filtration with that of $\mathcal N$ and $\mathcal N'$, giving <a href="#bl:frm:filtered-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:frm:filtered-comparison">[bl:frm:filtered-comparison]</a> for all $j$. The chain rule in the parameter coordinates gives the assertion about symbols. Terms arising from a change of canonical frame have order zero and do not change that assertion. ◻

</details>

One can also express the transversality in this proof as a non-characteristic condition. The characteristic variety of the normal crossing graph module is contained in the union of conormals to the graph strata involving $R$. A characteristic covector killed by $d(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}_Y\times\eta)$ has zero $Y$ component; its remaining component annihilates both the stratum tangent image and $d\eta(T_{B'})$. Transversality forces it to be zero. Finite flatness also makes the higher $\operatorname{Tor}$ groups of every graded filtration piece vanish. These are the two non-characteristic conditions of <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Saito1988](#ref-Saito1988), Section 3.5.1 and Lemmas 3.5.3, 3.5.5]; in relative dimension zero they give the same filtration index and right canonical factor as the explicit calculation above.

<a id="pm-h-18"></a>
#### The adjugate and the projective quotient

<a id="bl:frm:adjugate"></a>
For vector bundles of equal rank the adjugate of $d\eta$ defines a regular morphism $$\label{bl:frm:adjugate}
 \operatorname{adj}(d\eta):
 \eta^*T_B\longrightarrow\omega_{B'/B}\otimes T_{B'}.$$ Indeed $\det(d\eta)$ is a section of $\omega_{B'/B}$, so this is the usual determinant-twisted adjugate. It involves no division. If $v$ has zero symbol, applying <a href="#bl:frm:adjugate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:frm:adjugate">[bl:frm:adjugate]</a> to its tangent factor and then <a href="#bl:frm:filtered-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:frm:filtered-comparison">[bl:frm:filtered-comparison]</a> gives a section <a id="bl:frm:transported-symbol"></a>$$\label{bl:frm:transported-symbol}
 v'\in H^0(B',F_0\mathcal N'\otimes T_{B'})$$ with zero symbol. In local matrices this is just $d\eta\operatorname{adj}(d\eta)=\det(d\eta)\mathop{\mathrm{id}}$. The construction preserves the weight of $v$.

Suppose the horizontal image of $v$ is nonzero. Choose a point where that coherent-sheaf section is nonzero, and choose $\phi$ as above with branch divisor avoiding its image in $T$. On an unbranched neighborhood the tangent map is an isomorphism preserving the vertical tangent line. Faithful flatness there shows that the horizontal image of $v'$ is still nonzero.

Now take the diagonal quotient $$\overline X=(Y\times B')/\mathbb G_m\longrightarrow T'.$$ It exists as an associated algebraic variety: a local section of $B'\to T'$ identifies it with $Y$ times that base chart, and changes of section glue these products through the action on $Y$. It is smooth. The linearized ample bundle on $Y$ descends to a relatively ample bundle on this associated variety, so $\overline X$ is quasi-projective. The invariant divisor $D_h$ descends to a divisor $\overline D_h$, and the invariant closed support $Q'$ descends to $\overline Q$.

The support map $\overline Q\to T'$ is projective. Properness descends from the projective map $Q'\to B'$, and a descended relatively ample bundle gives projectivity. The quotient changes both the ambient dimension and the support dimension by one; thus $\overline Q$ still has codimension $b+1$. Define <a id="bl:frm:quotient-modules"></a>$$\label{bl:frm:quotient-modules}
 \overline{\mathcal M}=
 \mathcal H^{b+1}_{[\overline Q]}
       \bigl(\omega_{\overline X}(*\overline D_h)\bigr),
 \qquad
 \overline{\mathcal N}
       =\mathcal H^1\overline{\operatorname{pr}}_+
                          \overline{\mathcal M}.$$ Use the constant twist by $\dim\overline X$ in this formula. These are algebraic mixed Hodge modules, and the second has projective support over the projective base $T'$.

<a id="bl:frm:untwisting"></a>
<a id="bl:frm:quotient-filtration"></a>
Choose a frame of $J'$ on $U\subset T'$ and write a point of $Y\times B'|_U$ as $(z,t,u)$, with $z\in Y$, $t\in U$, and $u\in\mathbb G_m$. The coordinate change $$\label{bl:frm:untwisting}
 (z,t,u)\longmapsto(u^{-1}\!\cdot z,t,u)$$ turns the diagonal action $(z,t,u)\mapsto(\lambda\!\cdot z,t,\lambda u)$ into the action on $u$ alone. Since $Q'$ and $D_h$ are invariant, the support and divisor become products with $\mathbb G_m$ as well. Localization and support commute with this product. Their normalizations give $$\label{bl:frm:quotient-filtration}
 F_j\mathcal N'
   \simeq (p')^*F_j\overline{\mathcal N}
                     \otimes\omega_{B'/T'}.$$ To check the index, ordinary right smooth pullback of relative dimension one changes $F_j$ to $F_{j+1}$ and tensors with the relative canonical line. The normalized constant upstairs has one additional Tate twist. This cancels the index change: the normalized smooth inverse image is $[1](1)$, and gives exactly <a href="#bl:frm:quotient-filtration" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:frm:quotient-filtration">[bl:frm:quotient-filtration]</a>. See <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Schnell2014](#ref-Schnell2014), Section 30]. Relative direct image has the same relative dimension before and after this product, so its cohomological index stays one.

The relative canonical line in <a href="#bl:frm:quotient-filtration" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:frm:quotient-filtration">[bl:frm:quotient-filtration]</a> has invariant frame $du/u$. This frame is intrinsic as a relative form: a change of bundle trivialization changes it only by a form from the base. In the product description the vertical symbol is zero and the horizontal symbol is the pullback of that on $\overline{\mathcal N}$. In particular $F_{<0}\overline{\mathcal N}=0$, by <a href="#bl:frm:filtered-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:frm:filtered-comparison">[bl:frm:filtered-comparison]</a>, <a href="#bl:frm:quotient-filtration" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:frm:quotient-filtration">[bl:frm:quotient-filtration]</a> and faithful flatness.

Project $v'$ horizontally and use <a href="#bl:frm:quotient-filtration" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:frm:quotient-filtration">[bl:frm:quotient-filtration]</a>. A homogeneous section of weight $m$ on the nonzero vectors of $J'$ corresponds to a section twisted by $(J')^{-m}$. Explicitly, if local frames satisfy $e_j=g_{ij}e_i$ and a vector is $u_ie_i$, then $u_j=g_{ij}^{-1}u_i$; equality of expressions $u_i^m s_i$ forces $s_j=g_{ij}^m s_i$, the transition rule for a homomorphism from $(J')^m$. Thus the projected zero-symbol section gives <a id="bl:frm:ample-map"></a>$$\label{bl:frm:ample-map}
 (J')^m\longrightarrow
 \ker\left[F_0\overline{\mathcal N}\otimes T_{T'}
                   \longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_1^F\overline{\mathcal N}\right].$$ It is nonzero if the original horizontal projection was nonzero. For $m>0$ its source is ample, contradicting Lemma <a href="#bl:lem:symbol-vanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:lem:symbol-vanishing">10</a>. This proves assertion <span class="upright">(i)</span> of Proposition <a href="#bl:prop:frame-positivity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:prop:frame-positivity">12</a>. For $\ell=0$ there is no horizontal tangent direction and assertion <span class="upright">(i)</span> is immediate.

<a id="pm-h-19"></a>
#### The actual Euler action

It remains to prove assertion <span class="upright">(ii)</span>, which does not follow merely from the vanishing of the vertical symbol. Work on a bundle chart of $B$ and make the finite *étale* fiber cover $$\beta=u^r,\qquad u\ne0.$$ The generator <a href="#bl:frm:euler" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:frm:euler">[bl:frm:euler]</a> becomes $u\partial_u$. The coordinate change <a href="#bl:frm:untwisting" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:frm:untwisting">[bl:frm:untwisting]</a> again identifies the supported geometric construction with a product in the $u$ direction. This is an identification of the underlying differential modules, with their natural equivariance, not just an identification of coherent filtration pieces.

A weight-$m$ section is consequently $u^m$ times a section independent of $u$, in the invariant relative frame $du/u$. Right action on top forms is negative Lie differentiation. Since $du/u$ is invariant, $$(u^m s_1\otimes du/u)\,(u\partial_u)
       =-m(u^m s_1\otimes du/u).$$ The product direct image retains this action, proving <a href="#bl:frm:euler-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:frm:euler-action">[bl:frm:euler-action]</a> on the étale cover and hence downstairs. This completes the proof of Proposition <a href="#bl:prop:frame-positivity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:prop:frame-positivity">12</a>.

<a id="stmt-bl-frm-operator-order"></a>
<div id="bl:frm:operator-order" class="remark">

*Remark 14*. If $v=s_0\otimes\varepsilon$ and $\varepsilon=\sum_i c_i\partial_{t_i}$, then the relevant right operator is $$\sum_i(s_0c_i)\partial_{t_i}=s_0\varepsilon.$$ The coefficients precede the derivatives. Moving them to the other side would introduce a divergence correction; the displayed identity is simply associativity in the ring of differential operators.

</div>

<a id="bl:sec:lifting"></a>
### Infinitesimal lifting and section growth

We now apply the residue insertion and the two frame assertions to prove lifting through every finite order. Finite-cover invariants and the weight-zero part of the frame action will then return the lifted layers to $V$, where their positive twists force unbounded section spaces.

<a id="bl:lift:insertion"></a>
Retain the complex algebraic setup of the preceding sections. In particular, $I=\mathcal O_Z(-E)=y\mathcal O_Z$, the pullback weight of $y$ is $-1$, and $\Omega=\mathop{\mathrm{res}}(\sigma)$ has weight $a=r/q>0$. Write $$\label{bl:lift:insertion}
 \iota:R^1g_*\mathcal O_E\hookrightarrow
 R^1h_*\omega_R(D_h)=F_0\mathcal N\hookrightarrow\mathcal N$$ for the injections of Lemmas <a href="#bl:lem:residue-injection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:lem:residue-injection">8</a> and <a href="#bl:lem:lowest-piece" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:lem:lowest-piece">9</a>. Thus $\iota(e)=\Omega e$, with pullback and residue understood. If $F\in g_*\mathcal O_E$, the expression $\Omega Fe$ means $\iota(Fe)$: multiplication takes place on $E$, before the insertion. This convention does not require $F$ to come from $B$.

For all integers $j<d$, we push the underlying sheaf of complex vector spaces of $I^j/I^d$ along $|g|$ using the Zariski-topology convention of Section <a href="#bl:cov:obstruction-contract" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:cov:obstruction-contract">2.4</a>; negative powers mean invertible fractional ideals. No morphism from an infinitesimal thickening to $B$ is assumed. In contrast, multiplication by $y^j$ identifies $I^j/I^{j+1}$ with the coherent sheaf $\mathcal O_E$, with an equivariant weight shift. Its additive higher direct images are therefore the underlying sheaves of the usual coherent higher direct images under the projective morphism $g$.

<a id="stmt-bl-prop-lifting"></a>
<div id="bl:prop:lifting" class="proposition">

<a id="bl:lift:successive"></a>
**Proposition 15** (Infinitesimal lifting). *For every $k\geq1$, the truncation map $$\label{bl:lift:successive}
 g_*(\mathcal O_Z/I^{k+1})\longrightarrow g_*(\mathcal O_Z/I^k)$$ is surjective as a map of additive sheaves on $B$. More generally, for all integers $j<d$, the maps $$g_*(I^j/I^{d+1})\longrightarrow g_*(I^j/I^d),
 \qquad
 g_*(I^j/I^d)\longrightarrow g_*(I^j/I^{j+1})$$ are surjective. Each of these surjections is also surjective on sections over every affine open subset of $B$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-bl-prop-lifting"><summary>Proof of stmt-bl-prop-lifting</summary>

<a id="bl:lift:derivation"></a>
*Proof.* *The obstruction derivation.* We first prove <a href="#bl:lift:successive" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:successive">[bl:lift:successive]</a> by induction on $k$. Put $A_d=g_*(\mathcal O_Z/I^d)$. The obstruction to surjectivity at the $k$-th step is the connecting homomorphism $$\partial_k:A_k\longrightarrow R^1g_*(I^k/I^{k+1}).$$ The previous steps give a surjection $A_k\to A_1$. For $k>1$, its kernel is $g_*(I/I^k)$, by left exactness. This kernel lifts to $g_*(I/I^{k+1})$: multiplication by $y$ identifies the required surjection with the already established map $A_k\to A_{k-1}$. Consequently $\partial_k$ vanishes on that kernel. For $k=1$ the kernel is zero. After framing the target layer by $y^k$, we obtain in either case a uniquely defined map $$\label{bl:lift:derivation}
 \delta:g_*\mathcal O_E\longrightarrow R^1g_*\mathcal O_E,
 \qquad
 \partial_k(\widetilde F)=y^k\delta(F).$$ Here $\widetilde F$ denotes any local lift of $F$ to $A_k$; we suppress the index $k$ in the notation $\delta_k$ of Section <a href="#bl:cov:obstruction-contract" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:cov:obstruction-contract">2.4</a>.

<a id="bl:lift:leibniz"></a>
The map $\delta$ is a $\mathbb C$-linear derivation, where $R^1g_*\mathcal O_E$ is a module over $g_*\mathcal O_E$ by multiplication on $E$. To check this assertion, work locally on the base, choose lifts of $F,G$ to $A_k$, and represent them on an open cover along $E$ by ambient functions $F_\alpha,G_\alpha$. We use the Čech convention $(dc)_{\alpha\beta}=c_\beta-c_\alpha$. Both differences $F_\beta-F_\alpha$ and $G_\beta-G_\alpha$ are divisible by $y^k$. In $$F_\beta G_\beta-F_\alpha G_\alpha
 =F_\alpha(G_\beta-G_\alpha)
  +G_\alpha(F_\beta-F_\alpha)
  +(F_\beta-F_\alpha)(G_\beta-G_\alpha),$$ the last term vanishes modulo $y^{k+1}$, since $2k\geq k+1$. Divide by $y^k$ and restrict to $E$. The resulting cocycles give $$\label{bl:lift:leibniz}
 \delta(FG)=F\delta(G)+G\delta(F),\qquad \delta(1)=0.$$ The factorization just proved ensures independence of the chosen shorter lifts.

<a id="bl:lift:variable-identity"></a>
*The graph residue identity.* We relate this derivation to right differentiation on $\mathcal N$. Choose a coordinate chart in $B$, with coordinates $t_1,\ldots,t_b$, and put $e_i=\delta(t_i)$, applying $\delta$ to their pullbacks to $E$. For every local section $F\in g_*\mathcal O_E$, we claim that $$\label{bl:lift:variable-identity}
 \iota\bigl(\delta(F)\bigr)
   +\sum_{i=1}^b\iota(Fe_i)\,\partial_{t_i}=0
 \qquad\text{in }\mathcal N.$$ It is essential here that $F$ is arbitrary.

<a id="bl:lift:cochain"></a>
This can be checked at base germs. Shrink the base so that the induction provides lifts of $F,t_1,\ldots,t_b$ modulo $I^k$, and choose ambient representatives $F_\alpha,v_{\alpha i}$ on an open cover along $E$. Pull them back to $Y$, still writing $y$ for its pullback. On overlaps write <a id="bl:lift:overlap-functions"></a>$$\label{bl:lift:overlap-functions}
 v_{\beta i}-v_{\alpha i}=y^k u_{\alpha\beta,i},
 \qquad F_\beta-F_\alpha=y^k b_{\alpha\beta}.$$ The restrictions of $u_{\alpha\beta,i}$ and $b_{\alpha\beta}$ to $E$ represent $e_i$ and $\delta(F)$. The same assertions hold if representatives are specified only modulo $y^{k+1}$. On the product with the coordinate chart of $B$, put $\psi_{\alpha i}=t_i-v_{\alpha i}$ and $dt=dt_1\wedge\cdots\wedge dt_b$. In the full local-cohomology module $\mathcal M$ of Lemma <a href="#bl:lem:lowest-piece" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:lem:lowest-piece">9</a>, consider the zero-cochain of ordered generalized fractions $$\label{bl:lift:cochain}
 C_\alpha=
 \left[\frac{\sigma F_\alpha\wedge dt}
 {y^k\prod_i\psi_{\alpha i}}\right].$$ The brackets include the divisor support direction as well as the $b$ graph directions, in a fixed order. Poles along $D_h$ are allowed. The divisor pole bound for <a href="#bl:lift:cochain" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:cochain">[bl:lift:cochain]</a> is $R+D_h+k\widehat E$, so these classes belong to $\mathcal M$; they need not belong to $F_0\mathcal M$.

<a id="bl:lift:nilpotent-modulus"></a>
<a id="bl:lift:cech-expansion"></a>
Here is a calculation valid also at crossings and at components with multiplicity in $\widehat E$. Locally invert an equation of $D_h$ and let $r_0$ be a reduced equation for $R$. In this localization, $\sigma=\alpha/r_0$, where $\alpha$ is a regular top form, $r_0\mid y$, and the zero support of $y$ is exactly $R$. The divisor denominator in <a href="#bl:lift:cochain" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:cochain">[bl:lift:cochain]</a> is $r_0y^k$. In the quotient by this denominator, $$\label{bl:lift:nilpotent-modulus}
 y^{2k}=0,\qquad y^{k+1}=0
 \quad\text{in }\mathcal O_Y(*D_h)/(r_0y^k),$$ because $r_0\mid y$ and $k\geq1$. Thus the ideal generated by $y^k$ is square zero there. Localizations by $\psi_{\alpha i}$ and $\psi_{\beta i}$ agree canonically, since the two elements differ by a nilpotent element, and the exact first-order formula is $$\psi_{\beta i}^{-1}
 =\psi_{\alpha i}^{-1}
   +y^k u_{\alpha\beta,i}\psi_{\alpha i}^{-2}.$$ Multiplying these identities and using $F_\beta=F_\alpha+y^kb_{\alpha\beta}$, all terms containing two differences vanish by <a href="#bl:lift:nilpotent-modulus" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:nilpotent-modulus">[bl:lift:nilpotent-modulus]</a>. After dividing by the divisor denominator, the result in local cohomology is $$\begin{aligned}
\label{bl:lift:cech-expansion}
 C_\beta-C_\alpha
 ={}&\left[
  \frac{\sigma b_{\alpha\beta}\wedge dt}
       {\prod_j\psi_{\alpha j}}\right] \notag\\
 &+\sum_i\left[
  \frac{\sigma F_\alpha u_{\alpha\beta,i}\wedge dt}
       {\psi_{\alpha i}^{2}\prod_{j\ne i}\psi_{\alpha j}}
                    \right].
\end{aligned}$$ One can formalize this passage by first computing graph local cohomology over $\mathcal O_Y(*D_h)/(r_0y^k)$, and then mapping to divisor local cohomology by the fraction with denominator $r_0y^k$. Changing the graph equations by the indicated nilpotents gives the same localizations at the first stage. Divisor local cohomology is the direct limit of these denominator calculations, so the resulting identity is an identity in the original support module. This also explains why ordinary reduced-support division, without the factor $y^k$ in the modulus, would not justify the computation.

The coefficients remaining in <a href="#bl:lift:cech-expansion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:cech-expansion">[bl:lift:cech-expansion]</a> have only the simple divisor pole bound $R+D_h$. Their residues are respectively $\Omega\delta(F)$ and $\Omega Fe_i$ as Čech classes under <a href="#bl:lift:insertion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:insertion">[bl:lift:insertion]</a>. Indeed division by $y^k$ has already cancelled in the displayed coefficients. Changing a coefficient representative by a multiple of $y$ changes its product with $\sigma$ by a form regular in the $R$-direction after the localization, because $r_0\mid y$. Its residue is therefore zero. This is the product-residue map on the entire divisor, including its crossings.

<a id="bl:lift:right-sign"></a>
For completeness, the sign in <a href="#bl:lift:cech-expansion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:cech-expansion">[bl:lift:cech-expansion]</a> agrees with the right-module action. If $\eta$ is a form pulled back from $Y$, right differentiation of top forms is minus differentiation of their coefficients. Hence $$\label{bl:lift:right-sign}
 \left[\frac{\eta\wedge dt}{\prod_j\psi_{\alpha j}}\right]
       \partial_{t_i}
 =\left[\frac{\eta\wedge dt}
 {\psi_{\alpha i}^{2}\prod_{j\ne i}\psi_{\alpha j}}\right].$$ The rightmost-term map from $\mathcal M[0]$ to the relative right de Rham complex is a map of complexes and commutes with target differentiation. Compute its derived pushforward with an affine Čech refinement. The total differential of the zero-cochain <a href="#bl:lift:cochain" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:cochain">[bl:lift:cochain]</a>, which lies in relative degree zero, is its Čech coboundary, since there is no subsequent relative term. Its image in $\mathcal N=\mathcal H^1\operatorname{pr}_{B,+}
\mathcal M$ is zero. Equations <a href="#bl:lift:cech-expansion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:cech-expansion">[bl:lift:cech-expansion]</a> and <a href="#bl:lift:right-sign" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:right-sign">[bl:lift:right-sign]</a>, and the lowest-piece identification in Lemma <a href="#bl:lem:lowest-piece" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:lem:lowest-piece">9</a>, prove <a href="#bl:lift:variable-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:variable-identity">[bl:lift:variable-identity]</a>. No ambient lift of the map $g$ on a neighborhood of a whole fiber was used: the chosen ambient representatives and their overlaps suffice.

<a id="bl:lift:symbol-vector"></a>
*Vanishing of the obstruction.* Apply <a href="#bl:lift:variable-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:variable-identity">[bl:lift:variable-identity]</a> first with $F=1$, and take the first symbol. The tensor $v_k$, written $v$ at this fixed order, satisfies $$\label{bl:lift:symbol-vector}
 v=\sum_i\iota(e_i)\otimes\partial_{t_i}
 \in H^0(B,F_0\mathcal N\otimes T_B),
 \qquad \operatorname{symbol}(v)=0.$$ The restriction of $\delta$ to $\mathcal O_B$ factors through the algebraic Kähler differentials $\Omega_B^1$, by <a href="#bl:lift:leibniz" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:leibniz">[bl:lift:leibniz]</a>. Thus <a href="#bl:lift:symbol-vector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:symbol-vector">[bl:lift:symbol-vector]</a> is independent of coordinates and is a global algebraic tensor. The connecting map before the framing in <a href="#bl:lift:derivation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:derivation">[bl:lift:derivation]</a> is equivariant. Since $\lambda^*y=\lambda^{-1}y$, the framed map satisfies $$\lambda^*\delta(F)=\lambda^k\delta(\lambda^*F).$$ Consequently the derivation tensor has weight $k$: a coordinate’s weight in its coefficient is cancelled by the weight of its tangent vector. Multiplication by $\Omega$ adds $a$, so $v$ has weight <a id="bl:lift:positive-weight"></a>$$\label{bl:lift:positive-weight}
 m=a+k>0.$$

Proposition <a href="#bl:prop:frame-positivity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:prop:frame-positivity">12</a> forces the horizontal projection of $v$ to vanish. The vertical tangent line of $B\to T$ is freely generated by the invariant, nowhere-zero Euler field $\varepsilon=r\beta\partial_\beta$. Thus $v=s_0\otimes\varepsilon$ for a global section $s_0\in H^0(B,F_0\mathcal N)$ of weight $m$. Write $\varepsilon=\sum_i c_i\partial_{t_i}$. The identity with $F=1$, before taking the symbol, reads $$0=\sum_i\iota(e_i)\partial_{t_i}
   =\sum_i(s_0c_i)\partial_{t_i}=s_0\varepsilon.$$ Coefficients occur before the derivatives, so the last equality is associativity of the right action; no coefficient is commuted through a derivative. The Euler assertion of Proposition <a href="#bl:prop:frame-positivity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:prop:frame-positivity">12</a> now gives $0=s_0\varepsilon=-ms_0$, whence $s_0=0$. It follows that every $\iota(e_i)$, and hence every $e_i$, is zero. Return to <a href="#bl:lift:variable-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:variable-identity">[bl:lift:variable-identity]</a> for arbitrary $F$. It yields $\iota(\delta(F))=0$, and injectivity of <a href="#bl:lift:insertion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:insertion">[bl:lift:insertion]</a> gives $\delta(F)=0$. This proves the induction step and hence <a href="#bl:lift:successive" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:successive">[bl:lift:successive]</a>. The same argument includes $\dim T=0$, when there is no horizontal tangent direction.

<a id="bl:lift:coherent-kernel"></a>
*Signed powers and affine sections.* For any integers $j<d$, multiplication by the global meromorphic function $y^j$ identifies $$\mathcal O_Z/I^{d-j}\simeq I^j/I^d.$$ It transports the established surjections to all successive Laurent truncations. Iteration gives the surjection to the leading layer. Moreover, each single successive truncation fits into an exact sequence of additive sheaves $$\label{bl:lift:coherent-kernel}
 0\longrightarrow g_*(I^d/I^{d+1})
 \longrightarrow g_*(I^j/I^{d+1})
 \longrightarrow g_*(I^j/I^d)\longrightarrow0.$$ The kernel is coherent on $B$. On an affine open it has vanishing first cohomology, so the additive-sheaf cohomology sequence gives surjectivity on sections. Finitely many such steps give the asserted affine-section surjectivity to every leading layer. Only the kernels in <a href="#bl:lift:coherent-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:coherent-kernel">[bl:lift:coherent-kernel]</a> were assigned coherent $\mathcal O_B$-module structures. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-bl-lem-descent-growth"></a>
<div id="bl:lem:descent-growth" class="lemma">

<a id="bl:lift:descent-surjection"></a>
**Lemma 16** (Descent and section growth). *For $j\in\mathbb Z$, define the divisorial sheaf and its layer by $$J_j^V=\mathcal O_V\bigl(-\lceil jG/r\rceil\bigr),
 \qquad \mathcal B_j=J_j^V/J_{j+1}^V,
 \qquad Q_j=f_*\mathcal B_j.$$ The sheaves $\mathcal B_j$ are coherent on $S$, and for every $d>j$ the map $$\label{bl:lift:descent-surjection}
 f_*(J_j^V/J_d^V)\longrightarrow Q_j$$ is surjective as a map of additive sheaves on $T$. Here the left side is pushed along $|f|$ on its topological support $|S|$. Furthermore, $$h^0\bigl(V,\mathcal O_V(NG)/\mathcal O_V\bigr)\longrightarrow\infty
 \qquad\text{as }N\longrightarrow\infty.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-bl-lem-descent-growth"><summary>Proof of stmt-bl-lem-descent-growth</summary>

*Proof.* *The invariant layers.* Since $r$ is divisible by every $d_i$, we have $0<d_i/r\leq1$. Thus the coefficientwise difference $\lceil(j+1)G/r\rceil-\lceil jG/r\rceil$ has coefficients zero or one. A function in the reduced ideal of $S$ has order at least one along each $S_i$. The divisorial inequalities therefore show that this ideal carries $J_j^V$ into $J_{j+1}^V$. Consequently $\mathcal B_j$ is a coherent $\mathcal O_S$-module.

<a id="bl:lift:invariant-ideals"></a>
<a id="bl:lift:invariant-quotients"></a>
Let $\mu_r$ denote the deck group of the first, full root cover $\pi:Z\to P$. We have $$\label{bl:lift:invariant-ideals}
 (\pi_*I^j)^{\mu_r}=p^*J_j^V
 \qquad (j\in\mathbb Z).$$ Indeed an invariant meromorphic function in the full root algebra descends to $P$, even when that algebra is disconnected. At a prime over $p^{-1}(S_i)$, its downstairs order $n$ becomes $(r/d_i)n$, whereas $y$ has order one. Membership in $I^j$ is thus equivalent there to $$(r/d_i)n\geq j
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad n\geq\lceil jd_i/r\rceil.$$ At other primes it means ordinary regularity. The codimension-one test on the normal schemes proves <a href="#bl:lift:invariant-ideals" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:invariant-ideals">[bl:lift:invariant-ideals]</a>, also for negative $j$. The identification with the pullback divisorial sheaf can be checked on the local product charts of $P\to V$. Finite pushforward is exact, and averaging over $\mu_r$ is exact in characteristic zero. Hence $$\label{bl:lift:invariant-quotients}
 \bigl(\pi_*(I^j/I^d)\bigr)^{\mu_r}
   =p^*(J_j^V/J_d^V) \qquad(j<d).$$

*Descending the lifting maps.* To prove <a href="#bl:lift:descent-surjection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:descent-surjection">[bl:lift:descent-surjection]</a>, let $U\subset T$ be affine and put $B_U=B\times_T U$. The morphism $B\to T$ is affine, so $B_U$ is affine. Define the open neighborhood $$V_U=V\setminus\bigl(S\setminus f^{-1}(U)\bigr),\qquad
 P_U=p^{-1}(V_U),\qquad Z_U=\pi^{-1}(P_U).$$ The complement defining $V_U$ is closed in $S$, hence in $V$. Moreover $E\cap Z_U=g^{-1}(B_U)$ as topological spaces. Since the relevant quotients are supported on $E$, the affine-section assertion of Proposition <a href="#bl:prop:lifting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:prop:lifting">15</a> gives $$\Gamma(Z_U,I^j/I^d)\longrightarrow
 \Gamma(Z_U,I^j/I^{j+1})
 \quad\text{surjectively}.$$ Taking $\mu_r$-invariants and using <a href="#bl:lift:invariant-quotients" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:invariant-quotients">[bl:lift:invariant-quotients]</a> gives a surjection between sections over $P_U$ of the corresponding pulled-back quotient sheaves. These are canonically linearized pullbacks: their linearizations in <a href="#bl:lift:invariant-ideals" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:invariant-ideals">[bl:lift:invariant-ideals]</a> are the ones obtained by descending meromorphic functions.

We next take Laurent degree zero for the frame action. For a coherent sheaf $\mathcal A$ on $V_U$, the affine projection of the nonzero-vector bundle gives <a id="bl:lift:laurent-descent"></a>$$\label{bl:lift:laurent-descent}
 p_*p^*\mathcal A
   =\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb Z}\mathcal A\otimes L^{-n}.$$ The degree $n$ term has pullback weight $rn$. Sections commute with this direct sum on the quasi-compact separated open $V_U$; this also follows from a finite affine cover. The preceding surjection respects the displayed grading. Its degree-zero map is therefore surjective, and is exactly $$\Gamma(V_U,J_j^V/J_d^V)\longrightarrow
 \Gamma(V_U,\mathcal B_j).$$ These are the sections on $U$ of the two additive pushforwards in <a href="#bl:lift:descent-surjection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:descent-surjection">[bl:lift:descent-surjection]</a>. Affine opens form a basis, proving that assertion.

<a id="bl:lift:periodicity"></a>
*Counting the descended layers.* The sheaves $Q_j$ are coherent, since $f$ is projective. Ceiling arithmetic and the fixed identification $L|_S=f^*J$ give $$\label{bl:lift:periodicity}
 J_{j-r}^V=J_j^V\otimes L,\qquad
 \mathcal B_{j-r}=\mathcal B_j\otimes f^*J,\qquad
 Q_{j-r}=Q_j\otimes J.$$ Also $J_0^V=\mathcal O_V$ and $J_1^V=\mathcal O_V(-S)$, so $\mathcal B_0=\mathcal O_S$ and $Q_0=f_*\mathcal O_S\ne0$, because $S\ne\varnothing$.

Set $$\mathcal A_N=f_*(J_{-Nr}^V/J_0^V).$$ By <a href="#bl:lift:descent-surjection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:descent-surjection">[bl:lift:descent-surjection]</a> and left exactness, for each $-Nr\leq j<0$ there is an exact sequence of additive sheaves $$0\longrightarrow f_*(J_{j+1}^V/J_0^V)
 \longrightarrow f_*(J_j^V/J_0^V)
 \longrightarrow Q_j\longrightarrow0.$$ Thus $\mathcal A_N$ has a finite filtration whose coherent quotients, by <a href="#bl:lift:periodicity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:periodicity">[bl:lift:periodicity]</a>, are precisely <a id="bl:lift:positive-layers"></a>$$\label{bl:lift:positive-layers}
 Q_t\otimes J^n,\qquad 0\leq t<r,\quad 1\leq n\leq N.$$ The middle additive sheaves need not be coherent $\mathcal O_T$-modules; the following estimates use only these exact sequences and their coherent quotients.

<a id="bl:lift:higher-bound"></a>
Choose a common integer $n_0\geq1$ such that, for all $0\leq t<r$ and $n\geq n_0$, Serre vanishing and generation give $$H^i(T,Q_t\otimes J^n)=0\ (i>0),\qquad
 Q_t\otimes J^n\text{ is generated by global sections}.$$ Write $\ell=\dim T$. The cohomology sequences of the finite filtration show that all cohomology of $\mathcal A_N$ is finite-dimensional, that it vanishes in degrees greater than $\ell$, and, for $i>0$, that $$\label{bl:lift:higher-bound}
 h^i(T,\mathcal A_N)
 \leq\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}\sum_{n=1}^{n_0-1}
             h^i(T,Q_t\otimes J^n)=:C_i.$$ The constants $C_i$ are independent of $N$. Euler characteristic is additive for the same exact sequences, whence $$\chi(T,\mathcal A_N)
   =\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}\sum_{n=1}^N\chi(T,Q_t\otimes J^n).$$ For $n\geq n_0$ all summands are nonnegative, and the summand with $t=0$ is at least one: a nonzero globally generated sheaf has a nonzero global section. Therefore $\chi(T,\mathcal A_N)\to+\infty$. Together with <a href="#bl:lift:higher-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:lift:higher-bound">[bl:lift:higher-bound]</a>, this implies $h^0(T,\mathcal A_N)\to\infty$. This argument also covers zero-dimensional support of the $Q_t$, and $\ell=0$: an individual twist need not grow, since the number of positive layers already grows with $N$.

Finally $J_{-Nr}^V=\mathcal O_V(NG)$, and the definition of the additive pushforward identifies $$H^0(T,\mathcal A_N)
   =H^0\bigl(V,\mathcal O_V(NG)/\mathcal O_V\bigr).$$ This proves the claimed growth. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-bl-lem-descent-growth-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-bl-lem-descent-growth</summary>

*Proof of Proposition <a href="#bl:prop:complex-supported" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:prop:complex-supported">5</a>.* Apply Lemma <a href="#bl:lem:descent-growth" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:lem:descent-growth">16</a>. From the exact sequence $$0\longrightarrow\mathcal O_V\longrightarrow\mathcal O_V(NG)
   \longrightarrow\mathcal O_V(NG)/\mathcal O_V\longrightarrow0$$ we obtain $$h^0(V,\mathcal O_V(NG))
 \geq h^0\bigl(V,\mathcal O_V(NG)/\mathcal O_V\bigr)-h^1(V,\mathcal O_V).$$ The right side is unbounded. In particular some $\mathcal O_V(NG)$ has two linearly independent sections. On the integral variety $V$, their ratio is a nonconstant rational function, so the corresponding linear system has positive-dimensional image. Hence $\kappa(V,G)>0$. The actual linear equivalence $G\sim qA$ yields $\kappa(V,A)=\kappa(V,G)>0$, as required. ◻

</details>

<a id="bl:sec:abundance"></a>
### Abundance after nonvanishing

Over $\mathbb C$, the supported theorem closes the zero-Iitaka-dimension case once lower-dimensional abundance generates the whole reduced floor. For positive Iitaka dimension, the induction also needs effective minimal models of fiber adjoints, which need not be nef. We organize these two uses in one proposition, then verify its hypotheses in dimension four and under the all-dimensional premise.

Every effective representative below is effective up to actual $\mathbb Q$-linear equivalence. Ordinary log minimal models supply nef adjoints; semiampleness is the conclusion to be proved. The first lemma records exactly which section spaces survive passage to such a model.

<a id="pm-h-22"></a>
#### Minimal models and the whole reduced floor

<a id="stmt-bl-lem-abundance-model-comparison"></a>
<div id="bl:lem:abundance-model-comparison" class="lemma">

<a id="bl:eq:abundance-model-comparison"></a>
**Lemma 17** (Comparison with an ordinary log minimal model). *Let $(W,\Delta_W)$ be a projective lc rational pair and let $(V,\Delta_V)$ be a $\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt log minimal model. Use the log birational model convention in which divisors extracted on $V$ have coefficient one. On a common resolution $\alpha:U\to W$, $\beta:U\to V$, with compatible canonical divisors, $$\alpha^*(K_W+\Delta_W)
   =\beta^*(K_V+\Delta_V)+E,
 \qquad E\geq0,\quad E\text{ is }\beta\text{-exceptional}.
 \label{bl:eq:abundance-model-comparison}$$ Consequently the adjoints have the same section spaces in sufficiently divisible degrees, and hence the same Kodaira dimension.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-bl-lem-abundance-model-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-bl-lem-abundance-model-comparison</summary>

*Proof.* Set $E$ equal to the difference in <a href="#bl:eq:abundance-model-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:eq:abundance-model-comparison">[bl:eq:abundance-model-comparison]</a>. It is anti-nef over $W$, since $K_V+\Delta_V$ is nef. The discrepancy condition for a log minimal model gives $\alpha_*E\geq0$: only prime divisors of $W$ contracted by the model map can contribute, and their discrepancies improve. The negativity lemma therefore gives $E\geq0$.

At a prime divisor on $V$ which is also a divisor on $W$, the coefficient of $E$ is zero. At a prime divisor on $V$ extracted over $W$, it is the new log discrepancy minus the old log discrepancy. The former is zero and the latter is nonnegative. Its coefficient is thus at most zero, and the effectivity just proved makes it zero. This proves exceptionality over $V$.

For divisible $m$, adding the effective exceptional divisor $mE$ to $\beta^*m(K_V+\Delta_V)$ creates no additional sections. Indeed a rational function allowed by the former divisor has the required pole bounds at every prime divisor on the normal variety $V$, and therefore is a section downstairs; the converse follows by pullback. Birational pullback from $W$ preserves sections as well. These identifications prove the assertion. ◻

</details>

We may take the $\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt models in this lemma in the ordinary minimal-model existence statements below. A crepant dlt modification, when needed, has the same adjoint pullback and preserves nefness and all section spaces. The dlt modification theorem is, for example, <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[FujinoGongyo2014](#ref-FujinoGongyo2014), Theorem 2.8].

<a id="stmt-bl-lem-abundance-floor"></a>
<div id="bl:lem:abundance-floor" class="lemma">

**Lemma 18** (Semiampleness on the whole floor). *Assume abundance for projective lc rational pairs of dimension less than $d$. If $(V,C)$ is a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt pair of dimension $d$ and $A=K_V+C$ is nef, then the actual restricted rational line bundle $A|_P$, where $P=\lfloor C\rfloor$, is semiample. Its restriction to any reduced union of components of $P$ is also semiample.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-bl-lem-abundance-floor"><summary>Proof of stmt-bl-lem-abundance-floor</summary>

<a id="bl:eq:abundance-floor-adjunction"></a>
*Proof.* There is nothing to prove if $P$ is empty. Whole-floor dlt adjunction gives an effective rational boundary $\Delta_P$ for which $(P,\Delta_P)$ is semi-divisorial log terminal, in particular semi-log-canonical, and $$K_P+\Delta_P=A|_P.
 \label{bl:eq:abundance-floor-adjunction}$$ Here the equality is an adjunction identification of rational line bundles on the entire reduced scheme $P$; see <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[FujinoGongyo2014](#ref-FujinoGongyo2014), Remark 2.7]. In particular, it specifies the gluing along the double locus.

For clarity, if $\zeta:\coprod P_i\to P$ is the normalization, its pullback is the disjoint union of the adjunction formulas $K_{P_i}+\Delta_i=A|_{P_i}$. The conductor has coefficient one in $\Delta_i$ and the normalization pairs are dlt. The whole-floor statement includes the $S_2$ and nodal-in-codimension-one properties of $P$. The adjunction maps are pluriresidue maps: at a generic double point, the two branch residues have the usual opposite subsequent residues of an ambient logarithmic form. Taking an even divisible power removes the sign convention and gives the dualizing-sheaf gluing. Thus <a href="#bl:eq:abundance-floor-adjunction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:eq:abundance-floor-adjunction">[bl:eq:abundance-floor-adjunction]</a> is not merely a collection of componentwise equivalences; it identifies a divisible restricted invertible sheaf on $P$ itself.

Each $K_{P_i}+\Delta_i$ is nef and is semiample by the assumed lower-dimensional abundance. Semiampleness descends from the normalization of a projective slc pair by <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[FujinoGongyo2014](#ref-FujinoGongyo2014), Theorem 4.3, equivalently Theorem 1.5]. Applied to <a href="#bl:eq:abundance-floor-adjunction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:eq:abundance-floor-adjunction">[bl:eq:abundance-floor-adjunction]</a>, this gives generation of a power on the whole $P$. Restricting its evaluation map to a reduced closed union of components preserves surjectivity, which proves the last assertion. This also treats extra coefficient-one components meeting the selected union and its strata. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-23"></a>
#### The induction step

<a id="stmt-bl-prop-abundance-induction"></a>
<div id="bl:prop:abundance-induction" class="proposition">

**Proposition 19** (Abundance from effective models and lower-dimensional abundance). *Fix $d\geq1$ and assume the following two statements over $\mathbb C$:*

1.  *every projective lc rational pair of dimension at most $d$ whose adjoint has an effective $\mathbb Q$-linearly equivalent divisor has an ordinary log minimal model;*

2.  *every nef projective lc rational adjoint in dimension less than $d$ is semiample.*

*Then, for a projective lc rational pair $(X,B_X)$ of dimension $d$, $$D=K_X+B_X\text{ nef},\qquad \kappa(X,D)\geq0
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad D\text{ semiample}.$$ If $\kappa(X,D)=0$, then $D\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-bl-prop-abundance-induction"><summary>Proof of stmt-bl-prop-abundance-induction</summary>

*Proof.* We first treat Kodaira dimension zero, then the Iitaka fibers in the positive-dimensional case, and finally the lc centers. The second assumption is full lower-dimensional abundance; no nonvanishing hypothesis is imposed on the lower-dimensional restrictions in Lemma <a href="#bl:lem:abundance-floor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:lem:abundance-floor">18</a>.

*Kodaira dimension zero.* Choose $M\geq0$ with $D\sim_{\mathbb Q}M$. Suppose that $M\ne0$. Take a projective log resolution $p:W\to X$ of the pair together with $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}M$. Start with the strict transform of $B_X$, raise the coefficient to one along every strict transform of a component of $M$, and put coefficient one on every exceptional prime divisor. Call the resulting snc boundary $C_W$, and let $T_W\geq0$ be the sum of the coefficient increases on nonexceptional divisors. Using log discrepancies, we have <a id="bl:eq:abundance-raised-boundary"></a>$$\begin{split}
 K_W+C_W&\sim_{\mathbb Q}M_W,\\
 M_W&=p^*M+\sum_{E_i\text{ exceptional}}
       a(E_i;X,B_X)E_i+T_W\geq0.
 \end{split}
 \label{bl:eq:abundance-raised-boundary}$$ The pair $(W,C_W)$ is lc, and $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}M_W\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\lfloor C_W\rfloor$.

Raising the coefficients here preserves Kodaira dimension zero. To see this directly, a rational function in any divisible system $|mM_W|$ has, on $X$, poles only along $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}M$. There is a constant $c>0$, independent of $m$, such that the permitted pole order at every such prime is at most $cm$ times its coefficient in $M$: this is a finite list of positive coefficients. Increasing $c$ and clearing denominators identifies this rational function with a section of some $|nM|$. Since $M$ is effective and $\kappa(M)=0$, every such section is constant in the rational-function description of $H^0(X,\mathcal O_X(nM))$. Thus $h^0(W,mM_W)=1$ for every sufficiently divisible $m$, and $\kappa(W,K_W+C_W)=0$.

By the first assumption, take an ordinary log minimal model $(V_*,C_*)$ of $(W,C_W)$. On a common resolution $\alpha:U\to W$, $\beta:U\to V_*$ set $$M_*:=\beta_*\alpha^*M_W.$$ Lemma <a href="#bl:lem:abundance-model-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:lem:abundance-model-comparison">17</a> shows that $M_*\geq0$, $M_*\sim_{\mathbb Q}K_{V_*}+C_*$, and that the latter adjoint has Kodaira dimension zero. The support of $M_*$ lies in $\lfloor C_*\rfloor$: surviving components of $M_W$ already have coefficient one, and any component extracted over $W$ has coefficient one by the model convention.

Crucially, $M_*\ne0$. Otherwise $\alpha^*M_W$ would be $\beta$-exceptional. The nonzero effective divisor $$Q=(p\alpha)^*M\leq\alpha^*M_W$$ would then be exceptional over $V_*$ as well. But $Q\sim_{\mathbb Q}(p\alpha)^*D$ is nef. The negativity lemma applied to this effective exceptional, relatively nef divisor forces $Q=0$, a contradiction. This is the place where nefness of the original adjoint ensures that the effective representative survives on the new model.

Choose $q>0$ sufficiently divisible that $G=qM_*$ is Cartier and $G\sim q(K_{V_*}+C_*)$. Lemma <a href="#bl:lem:abundance-floor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:lem:abundance-floor">18</a> makes $\mathcal O_{V_*}(G)|_{G_{\mathrm{red}}}$ semiample. All the supported pair hypotheses now hold, with $G\ne0$. Proposition <a href="#bl:prop:complex-supported" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:prop:complex-supported">5</a> gives $\kappa(V_*,K_{V_*}+C_*)>0$, a contradiction. Therefore $M=0$ and $D\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$.

<a id="bl:eq:abundance-iitaka-bound"></a>
*A non-nef adjoint on an Iitaka fiber.* Put $k=\kappa(X,D)>0$. Resolve an Iitaka map of a sufficiently divisible multiple of $D$ and take its Stein factorization, using a simultaneous projective log resolution $\pi:W\to X$: $$f:W\longrightarrow Z,\qquad \dim Z=k.$$ With $\Gamma$ the strict transform of $B_X$ plus the reduced exceptional divisor, there is an actual equality <a id="bl:eq:abundance-nonnef-adjoint"></a>$$L:=K_W+\Gamma=\pi^*D+N,
 \qquad N\geq0\text{ exceptional over }X.
 \label{bl:eq:abundance-nonnef-adjoint}$$ In particular $\kappa(W,L)=k$. The resolved linear system also gives, for a positive integer $u$ and an ample rational divisor $A_Z$, $$uL\sim_{\mathbb Q}f^*A_Z+E_0,\qquad E_0\geq0.
 \label{bl:eq:abundance-iitaka-bound}$$ The ample divisor is obtained by pulling back the hyperplane bundle through the finite map in the Stein factorization.

Let $F$ be a very general smooth fiber. Generic smoothness for the finitely many horizontal snc strata and avoidance of the vertical strata give the log smooth lc pair $(F,\Gamma_F)$, with $$L|_F=K_F+\Gamma_F.$$ It has an effective rational representative: restrict any fixed nonzero divisible section of $L$, choosing $F$ outside the locus where the section vanishes identically. Moreover $\kappa(F,L|_F)=0$. Here is a justification that does not require $L|_F$ to be nef. Work with a Cartier multiple $\mathcal L$ of $L$ and clear <a href="#bl:eq:abundance-iitaka-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bl:eq:abundance-iitaka-bound">[bl:eq:abundance-iitaka-bound]</a> to write $v\mathcal L\sim f^*A+E_1$, with $A$ ample Cartier and $E_1\geq0$. Choose $F$ outside the countable union of the proper closed sets needed for base change of $f_*\mathcal O_W(t\mathcal L)$, for all $t>0$. If some fiber system has positive-dimensional image, then, after twisting that direct image by a sufficiently positive multiple $aA$, its global sections generate the fiber system at the general point of $Z$. Multiplying by sections of a further very ample multiple $bA$ gives a system in $$|t\mathcal L+(a+b)f^*A|$$ which detects both the base and a nonconstant fiber ratio. Its image has dimension at least $k+1$. Multiplication by the section of $(a+b)E_1$ embeds this system in $|(t+(a+b)v)\mathcal L|$, contradicting $\kappa(W,L)=k$.

Assume $s:=\dim F>0$. The pair $(F,\Gamma_F)$ has dimension $s=d-k<d$ and an effective adjoint, so it has an ordinary log minimal model $(F_*,\Gamma_*)$ by the first assumption. Its nef adjoint has Kodaira dimension zero by Lemma <a href="#bl:lem:abundance-model-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bl:lem:abundance-model-comparison">17</a>. The second assumption therefore makes it semiample, and hence $\mathbb Q$-linearly trivial. Notice that this applies lower-dimensional abundance on $F_*$; the divisor $K_F+\Gamma_F$ on the original fiber may be non-nef.

<a id="bl:eq:abundance-fiber-sandwich"></a>
On a smooth common resolution $\tau:\widetilde F\to F$ and $\mu:\widetilde F\to F_*$, the comparison gives <a id="bl:eq:abundance-fiber-comparison"></a>$$\begin{gathered}
 P+N_F\sim_{\mathbb Q}E_F,\qquad P:=\tau^*(\pi^*D|_F)\text{ nef},\\
 N_F:=\tau^*(N|_F)\geq0,\qquad
 E_F\geq0\text{ exceptional over }F_*.
 \end{gathered}
 \label{bl:eq:abundance-fiber-comparison}$$ If $H$ is the pullback of an ample divisor on $F_*$, the projection formula and effectivity yield the full inequality $$0\leq P\cdot H^{s-1}
       =-N_F\cdot H^{s-1}\leq0.
 \label{bl:eq:abundance-fiber-sandwich}$$ Since $H$ is nef and big, write $H\sim_{\mathbb Q}\epsilon A+T$ with $A$ ample, $\epsilon>0$, and $T\geq0$. Intersections of $T$ with products of nef classes are nonnegative.
